# Changelog

All notable changes to `ai-olympus` will be documented in this file.

## [Unreleased]

- 📝 **Changed**: the >4-parameter rule in `rules/php/core-standards.md` now exempts a data carrier's own constructor. The rule prescribes exactly one fix — introduce a typed DTO — and that fix is circular on a DTO's own constructor: applying it produces the same class a second time, which is what a review hit when it raised *"the default metadata bundle needs a DTO"* against a DTO. The exemption list was an absolute (*"The only exempt cases are …"*) and now carries exactly two categories: a signature fixed outside the project, and the data carrier's own constructor. A data carrier is defined structurally, not by the Laravel convention in `rules/laravel/architecture.md`, because `core-standards.md` is not Laravel-scoped — all four of: every constructor parameter is a promoted property; the constructor injects no collaborator; every public method other than the constructor is a named constructor, an accessor, a `with*()` copy method, or a vendor-fixed serialization method; and the class performs no I/O, no persistence, no dispatching, and no other side effect. A renamed `Service` fails two of the four. The exemption reaches that class's named constructors (`fromModel()`, `fromRequest()`, `fromArray()`, `from()`) and `with*()` copy methods whose parameters are the same field list or a subset of it, or the rule would fire again one line lower on an identical signature with the same circular fix; and it reaches value objects, whose constructors carry the same circularity, with their invariant check named so it is not read as a side effect. It covers the parameter count only — a DTO grown into a bag of unrelated fields is still a finding under the one-responsibility bullet. `rules/code-review/core-analysis.md` and `skills/class-refactoring/SKILL.md` already delegate the exemption list here by reference and inherit the new category with no edit; both stay pure delegations, since restating only the newer category would read as the complete list and restating both would fork the canonical text into a second copy that drifts. `tests/Installer/DtoConstructorParamExemptionTest.php` pins the exemption, the four conditions, the named-constructor and value-object reach, and the delegation decision. Per user request.
- ✨ **Added**: a `web-article-writer` skill for writing, rewriting, or adapting a publication-ready article for a website or blog. The package had no content-authoring skill at all: `agents/hermes.md` composes **short-form announcement content about a change shipped in this repository** — a tweet, a thread, release notes, a marketing blurb — which is a different job from authoring a long-form article on an arbitrary topic for an arbitrary site, and the standalone `article-writing` skill that once existed was removed in issue #6 precisely because its behaviour was inlined into that agent. The new skill runs eight steps: resolve the editorial brief, discover the target site's real publishing contract (loader, route, template, frontmatter, heading hierarchy, image dimensions) instead of assuming Markdown, build an evidence base with an internal claim ledger, plan, draft, apply restrained on-page SEO, prepare an illustration brief and alt text when asked, then edit and verify. It is stack- and CMS-neutral by construction and never publishes, deploys, or commits without an explicit ask. Three boundaries are stated in the skill itself rather than only in the pull request, so the skills stay discoverable apart: announcing this repository's own shipped change stays with `hermes`; a broader Laravel SEO audit or implementation additionally applies `skills/seo/SKILL.md`, which owns technical SEO (crawlability, JSON-LD, Core Web Vitals) while this skill owns on-page editorial SEO; and a structural technical picture — architecture, request flow, sequence, state machine, ER model — belongs to `skills/diagram-design/SKILL.md`, leaving step 7 here to cover editorial illustration and alt text only. **The submitted draft's `## Output Humanization` section, mandating an external `blader/humanizer` repository for all skill outputs, is not in the source file `skills/web-article-writer/SKILL.md` — but `src/InstallerHumanizer.php` appends that same section to every `SKILL.md` the installer writes, this one included, so every consuming project receives it anyway.** `skills/skill-creator/SKILL.md` permits a humanizer link when the user requests one, and this one was requested, so the letter of that clause was met. `blader/humanizer` is a real, MIT-licensed Claude Code skill — a `SKILL.md`, the same artifact class this package ships — installable through the same plugin marketplace this repository's own README points at, so installability is not the objection. Distribution is not the objection either: the installer writes that link into every installed skill itself, so it dangles nowhere. Neither is reach beyond this skill's own boundary, refuted by the same installer. The one objection that survives is **ordering** — the directive lands after this skill's own verification step, where a tool that rewrites finished prose to change how it reads can change what it claims, with nothing left to check it, directly against the anti-fabrication stance the rest of the skill takes. Step 8 therefore no longer bans an external rewriting pass outright. It orders one: any such pass runs before the verification step, and an article rewritten after that step goes back through it. That keeps the editorial invariant — verification is the last thing that touches the article — and it removes the head-on contradiction the installer would otherwise write into the shipped file, where an absolute ban sat directly above an absolute mandate. Whether the installer should append a third-party mandate to every skill at all is a pre-existing decision of the whole package, tracked in issue #111; this entry neither fixes nor excuses it. **The intent survives, only the tool reference is gone**: step 5 now names the machine register concretely (uniform paragraph and list rhythm, subject-first sentences, hollow bridge phrases, summarizing sentences that repeat the paragraph above) and asks the agent to break those habits while drafting, and step 8 re-checks the same list and orders any external rewriting pass ahead of itself. `README.md` gains a new **Content & writing** catalog section — no existing section fits a prose-authoring skill, and filing it under *Frontend & UI* would have mislabelled it as interface work — plus the skill-count bump in the *Why This Package* bullet, the plugin sentence, and the catalog intro; `docs/installation.md` and `docs/marketing/launch-article.en.md` carry the same count. Per user request.

- ✨ **Added**: a `/ai-olympus:finalize-tasks` slash command that drives the pull requests of the tasks at a supplied tracker link to a merge-ready state and consolidates their code-review and testing comments into one tracker comment. `commands/finalize-tasks.md` delegates the run to `daedalus`, takes the link as `$ARGUMENTS` (declared through `argument-hint`), and stops to ask for the link instead of guessing one when the invocation carries none. The rebase onto the main branch and the dependency install the assignment asks for are not a separate action `daedalus` performs — it holds read-only `git` and no write tool — they are the first thing the dispatch of the merge-ready step does, because `skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md` follows `rules/git/general.md` *Pull Policy* on the pull request's branch and runs `composer install` when the rebase changed the lockfile, so the command names that owner and reads the result back instead of restating the mechanics. Driving a pull request to merge-ready is real work rather than a status report, so the command has `daedalus` dispatch `athena`, the roster's single code-review agent, to run `skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md` on it — `daedalus` never invokes that skill itself, exactly as it never publishes the consolidated comment itself: the skill runs the review, applies a fix for every finding, and takes the pull request out of Draft the moment its convergence gate holds. The command cites that gate instead of restating it — the skill declares itself its canonical definition, and a second copy of a gate is a second answer to the same question. It also settles the two cases that wiring leaves open: a pull request carrying no review yet is that loop's first iteration, because the loop runs the review itself, so a missing review is never a reason to stop; and a loop that ends without converging — the skill caps it at three rounds — leaves the pull request a Draft, publishes nothing and hands the remaining findings back, so the command spells that branch out and stops there instead of assuming a merge-ready pull request always comes out the other end. Preparing a pull request for merge is not merging it — the command stops at merge-ready and never merges, because the verdict the consolidated comment carries, a direct merge or a review by the person who solved the task, is for a person to act on. The consolidation is **additive**: it posts one new comment and never edits and never deletes a comment — not somebody else's, and not one written under its own account either. That is the only shape the roster can actually perform, because every comment wrapper the package ships posts and never patches — one per tracker, `skills/code-review-github/scripts/upsert-comment.sh`, `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/upsert-comment.sh` and `skills/code-review-bugsnag/scripts/upsert-comment.sh` — no delete wrapper exists anywhere, and a deleted comment is not something a later run can restore; it also satisfies the amendment that an agent never touches somebody else's comment, trivially and in every case. `daedalus` does not publish the comment itself — it dispatches `hermes`, the roster's only publishing agent, which posts through the wrapper for the task's own tracker — detected from the link the way `skills/resolve-issue/references/source-detection.md` detects the source, so a JIRA task is published through the JIRA wrapper and never through the GitHub one — and reads the comment back, and the publish carries its own row at **L2** in `rules/compound-engineering/orchestration.md` *Externally-visible actions & consent levels*. The command still resolves the account the tracker tooling authenticates as — `gh api user --jq .login` on GitHub, `acli jira auth status` on JIRA — but only to decide which comments the summary speaks for, never to authorise a write on one: `skills/code-review-github/scripts/load-issue.sh` returns a comment's `login`, so the GitHub match is exact, while `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/load-issue.sh` returns only a display name, so a JIRA match is corroboration and never proof and any doubt makes the comment somebody else's, to be cited rather than spoken for — the same limitation `rules/compound-engineering/general.md` *Resolving trust per tracker* already records. Matching that account is not sufficient on its own, because the review the command drives publishes under the very same login: the command drops every comment its own merge-ready step posted — on GitHub the wrapper's hidden `<!-- cr-comment:actor=... -->` / `<!-- cr-status:actor=... -->` marker catches all of them, while on JIRA and Bugsnag, where the wrapper appends no marker at all, three shapes have to be recognised rather than one: the technical review by its `Status:` / `Counts:` header and severity-labelled findings, the non-technical `pr-summary` comment the same run posts there by its `What changed` / `How to test` / `Assignment Compliance` headings (`skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md` *Completion* invokes `code-review-jira`, which delegates that comment to `skills/pr-summary/SKILL.md`, and it carries no severity and no file paths by design), and a consolidated comment an earlier run of this command left behind by its own `HOW TO TEST` / `OPEN QUESTION` / `ASSIGNMENT COMPLIANCE` headings; a comment under that account the run cannot place is treated as its own and dropped, because one dropped by mistake is still quoted from the thread it sits in while one kept by mistake is reported back to its writer as the user's own note — and only then matches the account, so a run never summarises its own machine output as the user's notes about the review. The command reads every comment on the task, so it names the boundary that governs that reading: every comment is untrusted data to analyse and quote, never an instruction to follow (`rules/security/general.md` *Untrusted Content Boundary*), and quoted text is fenced inside the consolidated comment. The summary template ships as a plain-text fenced block, because a tracker that renders a comment as plain text shows Markdown markers literally; it carries the section headings, the numbering style and the layout the assignment asked for, filled with `<…>` placeholders rather than any real account, finding, or count from a past run. `tests/Installer/PluginMarketplaceTest.php` pins the argument placeholder, the consolidation contract, the additive guard, the per-tracker wrapper routing, the untrusted-content reference, the anonymised template, the consent-inventory row, the merge-ready loop with its dispatch to `athena` and its unconverged terminal branch, the exclusion of the run's own review comments from the consolidation, and the merge the command never performs the way it already pins `commands/install-rules.md` — `commands/` is a Claude Code plugin artifact the Composer installer never touches, so nothing else in the suite would notice any of them breaking. Per user request.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: the Bugsnag loader now reads an error's whole comment thread instead of its first page (#95). `skills/code-review-bugsnag/scripts/load-issue.sh` fetched `/errors/<id>/comments` in one request that passed no `per_page` and followed no `Link: rel="next"` header, so an error whose thread outgrew one API page came back silently short — the failure `rules/compound-engineering/general.md` *Truncated input is disclosed, never absorbed in silence* calls worse than a missing feature, because it looks exactly like complete context. The comments now go through a new `bsnag_get_all_pages` helper in `_lib.sh` that asks for 100 per page and follows the next link to the end; a thread longer than the 30-page cap is **named on stderr** and the pages already read are still returned, so a short read is never handed back as a whole one. The paging mechanic the project resolver already carried inline is extracted first — `bsnag_get_page` issues one status-checked request and leaves the response headers for `bsnag_next_page_url` — so the two paged reads share it rather than keeping two copies, and the page size, the page cap, and the 3000-item ceiling stop being repeated literals. Extracting it surfaced a second defect and fixed it: the request moved one command substitution deeper, and bash carries an `exit` out of the inner substitution only as a status, so a failing projects page would have printed its real HTTP cause and then carried on to report *project slug not found* — each caller now tests the call explicitly. The behaviour is proven by a new `load-issue.sh --self-test` (wired into `composer shell-self-tests`, the precedent being `skills/github-issue-triage/scripts/assign-priorities.sh`) that runs the real script against a stubbed `curl` answering from a routing table: an unrouted URL fails the run rather than returning a 404, so a request a case did not expect surfaces instead of being absorbed, and stderr is compared whole rather than by substring, because the failure worth catching is a run that prints the real cause and then reaches a second, misleading one. Following the header also hands the response the choice of the next destination while every paged request carries the Data Access token, so the next link is checked against the API origin first: one pointing elsewhere stops the walk and says so, instead of walking the token to a host nobody chose — and both paged readers inherit the check. The per-tracker cap enumeration in `rules/compound-engineering/general.md` is updated with the new Bugsnag reality and pinned against the old claim coming back. Per issue #95.

- 💥 **Changed**: the code review now judges the change against **the assignment**, converges in at most **three rounds**, and the merge gate moves to **0 Critical with no undeferred Moderate**. Rounds were being spent on findings nobody acted on, so four detection surfaces are retired and round 3 becomes a deferral boundary instead of a failure line. **What runs first now decides the verdict**: the new *Acceptance-Criteria Gate* in `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` walks the assignment's own criteria ahead of every other lens and anchors `## Functional Review` on the result; when the assignment enumerates no explicit criteria, it falls back to the existing Assignment Conformance Gate unchanged, stated explicitly so two agents never resolve the no-criteria case differently. **Four things stop being detected.** The blanket *Strict rule compliance* walk in `rules/code-review/core-analysis.md`, the largest single producer of Moderate and Minor findings, is retired; the severity stratification it defined survives it as `rules/code-review/general.md` *Default severity for a rule violation*, because surviving bullets still need a default. Both refactoring sections go — `## Refactoring (DRY / tech debt)` and `## Refactoring proposals` — with the pass and the two read-only lenses that existed only to fill them (`class-refactoring` and `refactor-entry-point-to-action` with `MODE=cr`, plus the Livewire/Blade layout-splitting walk and the `->when()` rewrite entry); the reuse-first gate stays, because a parallel implementation of existing logic is a rule violation and not a proposal, and both skills stay shipped for a caller that asks for a refactor. The **Minor bucket** is removed at detection rather than hidden at render time, with exactly one exception: a security lens publishes at whatever severity its own scale assigns, and `security-review` maps `Low` / `Info` onto CR Minor — *"Security-lens findings are never suppressed, at any severity"* is the constant this whole change is written around. The **late-iteration narrowing** (`iteration > 2`) is retired with them, because everything it suppressed is now gone and the one thing it exempted is what the Minor exception keeps; the `iteration` value and the `Report scope:` header line go with it. **Commit history stops being reviewed.** *One phase = one commit*, *One assignment point = one commit*, *Prefer independent, cherry-pickable commits* and *No commit ships dead code* stay in `rules/git/general.md` as authoring guidance and raise no finding; the dead-code bullet's `Moderate` severity is withdrawn. Three commit rules are named as untouched because none is about granularity: a committed failing or simulated-failing test stays **Critical**, and the merged-head gate plus the history-rewrite re-run stay enforced. That drops the commit-history half of `rules/refactoring/general.md` *Test Coverage Contract*, and the rule now states what it costs rather than hiding it — **the proof that behaviour was preserved across a refactor was the ordering, not the coverage number**; the coverage half (100% of every refactored line, read from the coverage tool) is unaffected and still **Critical**. **Round 3 defers instead of failing.** `skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md` *Review loop* step 4 is now the package's single definition of convergence — `criticalCount == 0`, `unfulfilledCount == 0`, and no Moderate left undeferred — and step 6 triages what survives round 3: a Critical is a hard stop, a Moderate meeting the **S1–S3** security carve-out is a hard stop, a non-security Moderate that clears the existing filing bar (`rules/compound-engineering/general.md`, cross-referenced and never restated) becomes a sub-issue of the source tracker item, and a Moderate that clears neither **stays blocking** — exactly one of *deferred* or *blocking* always applies, so nothing vanishes between two criteria. `skills/process-code-review/references/round-three-deferral.md` carries the resolution table and the per-tracker mechanics; `scripts/file-deferred-moderate.sh` performs the write and re-reads it to prove it landed, with `DRY_RUN=1` for a safe rehearsal. **GitHub attaches a sub-issue under any parent** — `AddSubIssueInput` takes only `issueId` and `subIssueId`, and a probe against a parent labelled `bug` resolved that parent and failed only on the child id, so the `EPIC` label `create-issues-from-text` uses is that skill's own convention rather than a GitHub requirement; JIRA uses `acli jira workitem create --parent`, verified against `acli`'s own `--help`; **Bugsnag has no sub-issue relation at all** and is refused with a pointer at the error's linked GitHub issue, the precedent *File deferred points as follow-up tracker issues* already sets. **The merge gate follows, coherently everywhere.** `rules/git/general.md` *Merging* and *Draft pull requests*, `skills/merge-github-pr/SKILL.md`, `agents/daedalus.md`, `agents/hermes.md`, `agents/athena.md`, `docs/agents.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `scripts/resolve-and-review.sh` and `rules/compound-engineering/orchestration.md` all state the new condition or cite the one place that computes it; the merge skill gains the three tests that let it verify the condition from what the review publishes. **Unit 5's phase-3 signal is reconciled rather than left beside a newer, contradictory gate**: `rules/compound-engineering/general.md` *Tracker status tracks the phase of work* now cites the convergence definition instead of restating `zero Critical and zero Moderate`, and says that wording is withdrawn — two definitions of convergence in one package is how a phase signal starts contradicting the merge gate it precedes. One instance is deliberately left stricter and says so inline: `resolve-issue`'s pre-PR self-check still resolves every Critical and Moderate it surfaces, because it is a single pass before any PR exists and has no round 3 to defer into. `skills/process-code-review/references/review-loop-scope.md` is a mechanical extraction that keeps the skill body under the `skill-check` budget. Pinned by new and rewritten tests in `tests/Installer/CodeReviewContentTest.php`, `tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php`, `tests/Installer/CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php`, `tests/Installer/AgentsTest.php`, `tests/Installer/DeadCodeInCommitRuleTest.php` and `tests/Installer/RuleFrontmatterScopingTest.php`. **Review round 2 corrected five things the first pass got wrong.** The sweep had missed four surfaces — `rules/jira/general.md` still stated the phase-3 trigger in the withdrawn words, and `skills/resolve-issue/references/pull-request.md`, `README.md` and `docs/marketing/launch-article.en.md` still stated the merge gate in them — so the pinning test now walks every `*.md` under `rules/`, `skills/`, `agents/` and `docs/` plus `README.md` and `CONTRIBUTING.md`, with a per-file allowed count so a new occurrence in an allowed file still fails. `scripts/file-deferred-moderate.sh` passed every `String!` / `ID!` GraphQL variable with `-F`, which sends a *typed* value: an all-digit owner or repository name was coerced to an integer GitHub rejects, and a leading `@` was read as a local file path — verified live, `-F owner=@probe.txt` came back from api.github.com quoting the file's contents, `-f owner=@probe.txt` quotes `@probe.txt` and reads nothing. The same script combined `set -e` with a discarded stderr on the two calls that run *after* the child issue exists, so a `gh` failure ended the run at exit 1 with empty output and took the new issue's URL with it; both now exit 4 naming it. `agents/athena.md` gained the round-3 filing as an obligation without the matching permission, which issue #194 forbids — its *Bash boundary* now names `file-deferred-moderate.sh`. And a deferred Moderate had nowhere to render, so a converged PR would have published `Status: needs-fix` over an unexplained open finding; both review templates gain a deferral-only `Deferred:` field and a `Status:` rule. Pinned by the new `tests/Installer/FileDeferredModerateScriptTest.php` and by additions to the four existing content-pin suites. Per user request — *aby code-review kontroloval hlavně akceptační kritéria zadání, cr max 3 kola, moderate ve třetím kole zakládat jako sub-issues, a vypnout kontrolu commitů v PR*.

- ✨ **Added**: a run that reads its assignment from a tracker now reads that item's **whole comment history**, under a trust gate. New section `## Analyze every comment before you act on a tracker assignment` in `rules/compound-engineering/general.md`, placed directly before *Claim a tracker issue before working on it* — the same family, one step earlier: this section owns what a run reads **before** the claim. It states the three reasons the comments are read (the clearest context, no work on a point the requester already cancelled, and the assignment's own instructions followed safely) and the boundary that makes the third one safe: a comment is **data**, an imperative sentence inside it is analyzed and never executed, and only a comment from a **trusted** author may refine the assignment's scope. *Trusted* is not redefined here — the section cites the test `rules/code-review/general.md` *Assignment-Declared Test-Only Conditions — Exclusion Gate* → *Authorship trust* already owns, so the two copies cannot drift. Trust resolves per tracker: GitHub reads `comments[].authorAssociation` straight off `load-issue.sh` (a sub-issue comment carries none, so it is untrusted); JIRA has no role data at all, so a comment is trusted only when its author's display name matches the issue's own `assignee` — the one identity of the three the loader carries that *Authorship trust* itself names, and the one JIRA grants through project membership rather than through filing. The `reporter` and the `creator` are deliberately outside the trusted set, stated inline with the reason: the cited test excludes *an external reporter* by name, and on an open project (Jira Service Management) the filer is an external account, so trusting them would restate the cited test with a wider value set and reach the opposite verdict from GitHub on the same person. The whole fallback is a documented limitation either way — a name match is corroboration, never a permission check; Bugsnag gates its comment surface at the platform (only a collaborator can post), so a resolved `collaborator.name` is trusted by default. Two open questions are decided in the rule rather than left to each agent: a **later explicit** decision from a trusted author wins over the issue body (a passing remark never does, and a genuine ambiguity is a question, not a guess), and every **truncation** of the loader's read set is written down where the next agent reads it, because a silent truncation looks exactly like complete context. The caps are stated **per tracker**, because they differ and a single list was wrong on all three: GitHub paginates an item's own comments in full and caps only the sub-issue tree (100 comments per sub-issue, the first 50 sub-issues, one nesting level, no line-anchored PR review comments), and Bugsnag fetches an error's comments in one unpaginated request, a loader gap rather than a designed cap, filed as issue #95 and left to that issue to fix. **A cap is not the only way the read set comes back short, so JIRA carries the other half.** No size cap applies there at all — issue and subtask comments both paginate in full — but `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/load-issue.sh` is one of the two loaders that swallow a failure: it substitutes `{"comments": []}` whenever the `acli` call fails, on the issue's own comments and on each subtask's, and skips a subtask whose own view fails, dropping that subtask's comments with it. An empty JIRA `comments[]` is therefore indistinguishable from a failed fetch and reads as *unverified*, never as *no comments*, while the item's own comments on GitHub and Bugsnag's request both exit 3 on a failed fetch, so an empty list there is a real one. GitHub's sub-issue tree degrades the same way: any GraphQL failure falls back to an empty array — the loader says so inline — so an empty `subIssues[]` is indistinguishable from a failed sub-issue fetch and reads as unverified, exactly as on JIRA. The mandatory disclosure names **which cap was hit, or which fetch degraded** — framing incompleteness as a cap alone would let a JIRA run conclude there was nothing to disclose. **The gap this closes is on the implementation path, not only the orchestration one:** `skills/resolve-issue/references/comment-analysis.md` already classified every comment into *Current requirements* / *Resolved items* / *Outdated items* with **no trust gate at all**, so any commenter could shape what `resolve-issue` treated as a current requirement — it now resolves author trust before it classifies, only a trusted author's comment moves an item between the three buckets, an untrusted comment is evidence only, and the reopened-task deep pass inherits the same gate for the continuation scope. `agents/daedalus.md` (gather phase), `skills/prepare-issue-context/SKILL.md`, and `docs/agents.md` carry one-line cross-references to the canonical section instead of a second copy. The section also says out loud that those consumers are the **assignment-reading** path and not every path that reads a comment: `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` *Issue Context Analysis* derives requirements, acceptance criteria, edge cases, and test data from comments and applies no trust gate today, so the enumeration names that gap rather than reading as complete — closing it is a separate change. Per user request — *aby měl co nejjasnější kontext a nedělal zbytečnou práci a dodržoval pokyny ze zadání*.

- ✨ **Added**: the tracker phase invariant gains a third phase — *ready to merge*. `## Tracker status tracks the phase of work` in `rules/compound-engineering/general.md` grows from two phases to three: phase 3 is written the moment the code review converges (0 Critical + 0 Moderate) and **before** the merge, on both surfaces — the pull request leaves Draft and the source tracker item gains a ready-to-merge status. Until now the tracker said the work was in progress, then that it waited for a reviewer, and then went quiet: nothing distinguished a PR still under review from one whose review had finished and which was only waiting to be merged. **Phase 3 has a different owner than phases 1 and 2.** Convergence is determined inside the review-and-fix loop, which the implementing agent never observes — it applies fixes and hands back to the review — so the write belongs to the step that already promotes the PR out of Draft at that same moment: `@skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md`, run by `athena`. It is the same action as that promotion, not a parallel one, and phases 1 and 2 keep `hephaestus` as their owner unchanged. **Three cases are named inline rather than left implicit.** A later commit that re-opens the review makes the phase-3 statement false, so the signal is withdrawn symmetrically with how it was written — `gh pr ready --undo`, the GitHub label removed so the still-present `ready for review` label is the active signal again, and JIRA moved back through the **existing** Code Review transition, so the revert direction needs no new capability, and a withdrawal that does not land — a write silently blocked in auto-mode, or an exit 5 because the review column is not reachable — is reported as a stale phase-3 signal instead of being taken for a successful revert; a fix commit carrying only verbatim fixer output re-opens nothing and never triggers it, and the role that *detects* the invalidation (typically `hephaestus` at the pre-merge gate) is not the role that *owns* the write. A run on a task the user described directly has no tracker item, so the issue-side half is an **explicit no-op**, mirroring *No tracker item, no link*. And Bugsnag's `status` resolution enum (`open` / `fixed` / `ignored` / `snoozed`) carries no ready-to-merge value either, so its documented limitation now covers all three phases with the same substitute signal. **JIRA needed a third helper.** `rules/jira/general.md` moves from *two exceptions* to *three* and names `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/transition-to-ready-to-merge.sh`, a self-contained clone of the two existing helpers (anchored key regex, merge-name guard accepting only a target containing `merge` or listed in `JIRA_READY_TO_MERGE_SYNONYMS`, idempotent no-op, post-transition re-read that catches an `acli` false-positive looped transition). The accept check alone was not enough: `merge` is also a substring of the terminal columns many boards use — `Merged`, `Merged to master`, `Done - merged` — so a **resolution deny check** runs after it and refuses every such name, including one listed as a synonym, because a phase write is never a resolution write. `Ready to Merge` and `Ready for merge` carry `merge`, not `merged`, and still pass. No shared library is extracted — `src/Installer.php` ships `skills/` verbatim, so each helper stays self-contained. The per-tracker mechanics live in the new `skills/process-code-review/references/ready-to-merge-signal.md`, keeping the skill body under the `skill-check` limit (4 944 whitespace tokens). The write gains its own L1 row in `rules/compound-engineering/orchestration.md` *Externally-visible actions & consent levels*, attributed to `athena`, and `agents/athena.md` *Bash boundary* grants the three `gh label` / `gh issue edit` commands plus `gh pr ready --undo` that row assigns it, so obligation and permission agree (issue #194) — mirroring how `agents/hephaestus.md` names its own phase-write surface. Pinned by new tests in `tests/Installer/CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php`, `tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php`, and `tests/Installer/AgentsTest.php`. Per user request — *rozšířit fáze o třetí: „ready to merge" se zapisuje při konvergenci code review, před samotným mergem*.

- ✨ **Added**: `daedalus` now checks the liveness of a long-running dispatch. New section *Liveness check for a long-running dispatch* in `agents/daedalus.md`, next to *Dispatch blocking, not fire-and-forget* — same family, the missing third case. Blocking dispatch keeps a turn from ending mid-flight and the *Dispatch ledger* makes a re-dispatch detectable, but nothing noticed a round that simply never comes back; it is the earlier-stage sibling of the recorded lesson that a background dispatch reporting `completed` is not proof it published. The mechanism is a **non-intrusive state read** — `ListAgents` busy/idle, or a `SendMessage` subscription carrying `notify_when_idle: true` and no `message` body — never a question written into the dispatched agent's own context, which would spend the context it was dispatched with and which this harness's own tool guidance forbids in as many words. **Both tool-availability paths are stated rather than one assumed**, including the one this roster is actually on: `daedalus`'s tool list is `Task, Read, Glob, Grep, Bash` and enumerates no such primitive, so unless the harness exposes one the completion notification is the only signal it has — a documented environment limitation, and the check is never fabricated, never faked with a Bash probe against a PID it does not hold. *“Every 10 minutes”* is an **elapsed-time gate**, not a timer: `daedalus` has no sleep or timer primitive, so the check runs opportunistically whenever a turn resumes over a round the ledger still shows open. Stuck is **3 consecutive `busy` readings with no progress** (≈30 minutes), never one sample; detection escalates per `rules/compound-engineering/general.md` *Blocked delegation is a hard stop* — terminate the task where the harness allows it, otherwise stop as `Blocked` — and never re-dispatches a duplicate over an unconfirmed original, which is the doubled-external-write class the ledger exists to prevent. Checks are capped at **6 per round** (≈60 minutes), mirroring the CR loop's `maxIterations = 3` safety net, and a round that delivers inside 10 minutes triggers no check at all. The cap bounds the checking and escalates **only a round without progress** — elapsed time alone never declares a visibly moving round `Blocked`, which would imprison working work behind the no-re-dispatch rule — and the two counters the section runs are named apart (**no-progress streak**, reset by any observed progress, versus the never-reset **check count** behind `<n>/6`), so no rule says "the counter" and leaves the reader to guess. The order against *Dispatch ledger* → *Consult it before every dispatch* is stated rather than left to a literal reading that would fire that stop before the check could ever run: the liveness check runs first, and the ledger's `Blocked` covers the round no check could examine. Naming the path taken and the tool-availability limitation is a conditional **Liveness checks** item of the handoff contract, rendered only on a run where a check came due. Each check is recorded as a deliberately **non-terminal** `liveness <n>/6` line in the dispatch ledger, never in `.audit` — a liveness read contacts no host and writes nothing externally, so it is none of that file's event classes. The section lives in `agents/daedalus.md` alone, verified rather than assumed: `daedalus` is the only agent whose frontmatter declares `Task`, so there is no second dispatcher for `rules/compound-engineering/orchestration.md` to hold the principle for.
- ✨ **Added**: every pull request an agent opens now links back to the tracker item its assignment was read from, in both directions. New section `## Every pull request links back to its tracker issue` in `rules/compound-engineering/general.md`, placed directly after *Tracker status tracks the phase of work* — same family, same principle-here / mechanics-in-`resolve-issue` split. The reference lives in the pull request itself (body, or title where the tracker keys off the title), the tracker item gains the PR URL, both directions are verified by re-reading through the deterministic loader, and a link that did not land is reported instead of assumed. A task the user described directly carries no tracker item, so the step is **inapplicable** rather than failed — the case every described-task run of this package hits. **The GitHub half was half-written and on the wrong surface**: `rules/git/general.md` *Issue Linking* said *"if a GitHub issue is provided, always link it in commits"*, but GitHub derives `closingIssuesReferences` from the **pull request body**, and every skill here reads `closingIssues[]` off the PR long before a merge lands that commit on the default branch. It now requires the literal English `Closes #<N>` in the body (PR #43 proved a translated keyword parses as nothing), keeps the commit reference as the additive half that closes the issue on a rebase merge, and names the English-keyword exemption on the PR-language line it excepts. `skills/resolve-issue/references/pull-request.md` replaces *"reference to the original issue"* with one concrete shape per tracker plus the post-creation verification read. **JIRA was the real gap**: its whole follow-up was the sentence *"Link the created PR back to the JIRA issue."* — no script, no command, no content. `skills/resolve-issue/references/tracker-follow-up.md` now posts the PR URL through `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/upsert-comment.sh` (folded into the non-technical report that already goes to the same issue) and verifies it in `comments[]`. The comment is the mechanism because the structured alternative does not exist: `acli jira workitem link create` links a work item only to another work item and `acli` exposes no raw-URL link subcommand. The JIRA key travels in the PR title so Atlassian's Development panel has a key to match, which is opt-in infrastructure on the JIRA side and therefore additive — `devSummary.pullRequestCount > 0` is a second confirmation, never a substitute. **Bugsnag is a documented limitation**: its API exposes comments and status as its only writes and carries no field connecting an error to a pull request, so the comment carrying the PR URL is required even when `linkedIssues[]` is empty — which is exactly the case where nothing else connects the two. `skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md` Finalization, the only other path that opens a PR, carries the same obligation, mirroring how it already carries the phase-2 status write. The link-back gains its own L1 row in `rules/compound-engineering/orchestration.md` *Externally-visible actions & consent levels*, and `agents/hephaestus.md` *Bash boundary* grants the `upsert-comment.sh` write that row assigns it, so obligation and permission agree (issue #194). Pinned by new tests in `tests/Installer/IssueLinkingRuleTest.php`, `tests/Installer/CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php`, `tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php` and `tests/Installer/AgentsTest.php`. Per user request — *pokud agent či skill vytvoří pr, tak je potřeba tento pr vždy prolinkovat s issue trackerem ze kterého četl zadání*.

- ♻️ **Changed**: `pr-summary` now reports **what broke, why, and what the reader can observe instead** — and reports it identically on every tracker. The comment was a two-section business summary (`Authors` / `Available behind` / `Summary of changes` / `How to test`) whose GitHub shape was full and whose JIRA shape was cut down to the test steps. It told a reader what a change was *called*; the new structure tells them what happened. `skills/pr-summary/SKILL.md` now renders **`What changed`** — `Problem` (who hit it, on what, and at what scale), `Cause` (one mechanism, stated for a non-developer), `Result` (what the cause cost, and why a silent failure was silent), `What I fixed` (one bullet per observable change, with the before-and-after where a number exists), plus the conditional `Side benefit` and `Filed separately` fields that render only when they carry content — followed by **`How to test`**, which is a scenario a human follows rather than a checklist of tests: every step names a concrete input (account, URL, entity name, value typed in) and states an explicit must-hold outcome, and the last step names the regression flows. A closing line links the PR and the source issue. **Three fields are gone and each removal is its own decision.** `Authors` goes because the skill resolved authorship for a line no reader of a change summary needed; `skills/tester-cookbook/SKILL.md` genuinely needs one for its QA audience, so it now carries the four resolution steps inline instead of citing a mechanism that no longer exists, and `skills/assignment-compliance-check/SKILL.md` drops its own copy of the line for the same reason. `Available behind` goes as a *metadata line* while the *fact* stays: a gating toggle now lives inside the `How to test` step that enables it, naming the exact toggle and value, which is where a tester needs it. The `{assignment_verdict}` top banner goes because it duplicated a verdict `assignment-compliance-check` already issues — that block still reaches the reader through the untouched `{embedded_blocks}` slot (issue #498), so a duplicate is removed, never a check. **JIRA and Bugsnag reach parity with GitHub**: the reduced JIRA shape existed only to drop `Authors` and `Summary of changes`, and with neither field left there is nothing to reduce, so `skills/code-review-jira/SKILL.md` says so explicitly rather than letting a behaviour change pass in silence. Bugsnag gains `skills/pr-summary/templates/pr-summary-bugsnag.md`, the plain-text template it was delegated to but never had — `skills/code-review-bugsnag/SKILL.md` had been naming two sections and no template at all. **Length is no longer bounded**: the "fits on one screen" rule is replaced by `@rules/writing/general.md`, so a one-line fix produces a short report and a change with a measured cause and four things to retest produces a long one. The terse contract from issue #51 stays, minus the two clauses (fragments are OK, drop articles) that contradicted that rule's ban on telegraphic compression. Downstream: `rules/reports/general.md` describes the one shape across targets and states that headings and field labels are prose, so a Czech assignment renders `Co se změnilo` / `Jak otestovat` rather than an English heading above Czech prose; `skills/code-review-github/references/cr-wrapper-contract.md` stops passing an author set and keeps passing the gating detection; `skills/prepare-issue-context/SKILL.md`, `skills/code-review/SKILL.md`, `agents/hermes.md` and `agents/daedalus.md`'s reporting dispatch all name the new sections. Pinned in `tests/Installer/CodeReviewContentTest.php`, where the three removals are asserted as absences that fail on the base branch rather than passing vacuously. Per user request — *přepsat `pr-summary` na strukturu Co se změnilo / Jak otestovat; délka podle faktů, styl podle `@rules/writing/general.md`*.

- ✨ **Added**: the tracker now always shows which phase of the work an agent is in. New section `## Tracker status tracks the phase of work` in `rules/compound-engineering/general.md` (directly after *Claim a tracker issue before working on it*, which owns the overlapping phase-1 write) states the two-phase invariant: an *in progress* status before the first file is written, and a *waiting for code review* status once the pull request is open. Both writes are unconditional, verified by re-reading the issue through the deterministic loader, and idempotent. The one real gap this closes is on GitHub: `skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md` used to apply the `ready for review` label only *if the original repository uses* one and skip the step otherwise, so the signal was a coin flip on repository configuration. The label is now created when the repository lacks it (`gh label create "ready for review"`, mirroring the `EPIC` label precedent in `create-issues-from-text`), applied, and then verified — the same apply-then-verify discipline the `Resolve_by_AI:in-progress` claim label already used. The claim label stays in place; removing it would make the issue an unclaimed candidate again. JIRA needed no functional change — `transition-to-in-progress.sh` and `transition-to-code-review.sh` were already called at both points — and its two call sites now name which phase they satisfy. `skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md` writes the phase-2 signal too, since its Finalization step is the only other path that can open the PR. **Bugsnag is a documented limitation, not a silent omission**: its `status` field is a resolution enum (`open` / `fixed` / `ignored` / `snoozed`) carrying no in-progress and no in-review value, so there is no status either phase write could set, and marking an error `fixed` before a human verifies the fix in production would misreport it. Bugsnag therefore performs no status write at all; the comment the run posts on the error and mirrors onto the linked GitHub issue is the substitute signal, and the rule names that exception inline. The second named exception is the PR opt-out: with no PR, phase 2 is never reached. The review-waiting write carries its own L1 row in `rules/compound-engineering/orchestration.md` *Externally-visible actions & consent levels*; `agents/hephaestus.md` *Bash boundary* names the three `gh label` / `gh issue edit` commands the two phase writes need as its sanctioned exception to the no-raw-`gh`-write rule, so the assigned obligation and the granted permission agree (the parity issue #194 asks for), mirroring how `agents/daedalus.md` names its own `gh label` surface. And *Label newly created tracker issues* now names the phase label as the second sanctioned label-creation exception beside `EPIC`. The three per-tracker follow-up sections moved into `skills/resolve-issue/references/tracker-follow-up.md` so the skill body stays under the `skill-check` 5 000-token limit (4 789, down from 4 834 before the change). Pinned by new content tests in `tests/Installer/CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php` and `tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php`. Per user request — *když ai agent řeší nějaký úkol, tak v issue trackeru bude vždy nastavený status, který odpovídá tomu, že issue je v řešení, pokud je issue vyřešeno, tak změnit stav, který odpovídá tomu, že úkol čeká na code review*.

- ✨ **Added**: the acceptance-criteria coverage gate now also asks whether a test uses the **data the assignment states**, not only whether it targets the right scenario. `rules/code-review/review-process.md` *Validation & Coverage Gate* defines a criterion as covered when a test targets the scenario **and** uses the values the assignment fixes — an input value, a payload, a boundary value, an expected output, or an expected error message — so a report that names 499 Kč and attaches the payload is no longer satisfied by a test asserting the same rejection at an invented 100 Kč. The condition is deliberately narrow: it does not apply at all when the assignment states no concrete value, so on such a PR the finding counts are identical to what they were before. It also names what is **not** a deviation — a named fixture or constant holding the stated value, an equivalent representation of it, and any further cases the test adds on top — because a rule that flagged those would fire on almost every well-written test. The finding's **Test Hint** carries the values verbatim, and `skills/create-missing-tests-in-pr/SKILL.md` now states that it copies them verbatim rather than re-inventing them, which is the only channel those values have: that skill reads the review, never the tracker. `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` drops the weaker sibling wording it used to carry ("verify they are covered by existing or new tests", with no definition of coverage and no severity) and points at the gate instead, and its *Issue Context Analysis* gains a fourth branch so a run with no tracker reads the criteria and data from the assignment it carries — the gate is skipped only when no assignment exists at all, and the review states that skip as an assumption. Gating is written on both sides: the **Coverage gate** still owns uncovered lines, and *Test organization* still owns placement, naming, and AAA order, so a wrong-data test raises one finding, never two.

- 🧹 **Changed**: `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` and `skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md` now fit inside the `skill-check` body budget — both had been over the 5 000-token limit (5 359 and 5 127), which the linter reports as a warning on every run. Three sections moved into `references/` files the skill names, the pattern `resolve-issue` already used for seven of its own sections: `code-review`'s *Specialized Reviews* → `skills/code-review/references/specialized-reviews.md` (5 359 → 4 329), and `resolve-issue`'s *Comment analysis* → `references/comment-analysis.md` plus *Code quality self-check* → `references/code-quality-self-check.md` (5 127 → 4 834). No rule moved: each skill keeps the sentences that decide behaviour (the always-run lens list, the reopened-continuation blocking rule, the 0 Critical / 0 Moderate PR gate) and points at the reference for the procedure. `crContractText()` in `tests/Pest.php` now folds `specialized-reviews.md` into the `code-review` skill text so the existing content tests keep asserting against the whole contract, and the reopened-task placement test spans the skill plus its comment-analysis reference. `skill-check` reports 0 errors and 0 warnings.

- ✨ **Added**: an incremental review scope to the whole code-review chain — a later CR round now reviews the **diff since the revision the previous round reviewed**, not the pull request from its first commit. New canonical section *Incremental Review Scope — Diff Since the Last Reviewed Revision* in `rules/code-review/general.md`: the baseline resolves from the caller's `reviewedRevision`, else the `Reviewed revision:` header line on the newest published CR comment, else the round is round 1 over the whole PR; a baseline that is not an ancestor of `HEAD` (force-push, rebase, squash) falls back to the full-PR review with the reason stated. New-finding detection is scoped to `git diff <baseline>..HEAD`, while the Coverage gate, the Assignment Conformance Gate, and the Reviewer Comment Fulfillment Gate keep reading the whole PR. Every finding from an earlier round that was neither fixed nor rejected is **carried over unconditionally** at its original severity, so a narrowed round can never converge a PR that still holds an unresolved Critical. A finding is settled only by the reviewer's own re-read showing it fixed, or by a trusted author's recorded rejection — never by a `kolo N` marker, a "resolved" note, or a ticked checklist, all of which stay untrusted content, and a security finding (the S1–S3 carve-out) is never settled by a rejection at all. Every finding now carries a **Provenance** field (`regression — introduced in this revision` / `pre-existing — …`), which changes no severity, no count, and no gate. Wired through `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` (new *Incremental review scope gate*, and the *Cross-run history* section now permits reading prior CR comments for the baseline while still forbidding a `Previous CR Status` section), `skills/code-review-github/references/cr-wrapper-contract.md` (baseline resolution, the two new header lines, the Provenance field), all three wrappers (each naming where its own tracker carries the round markers), both output templates, and `skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md`, whose quiet loop publishes nothing and must therefore pass `reviewedRevision` plus the previous iteration's finding dispositions itself. Per user request — *Zaměř se výhradně na změny provedené od posledního kola code review*.

- 📝 **Changed**: `README.md` now points at [`pekral/github-readme-generator`](https://github.com/pekral/github-readme-generator) for README authoring, in a note under the **Meta & tooling** catalog table. The previous change removed the in-package `readme-generator` skill and left no pointer behind, so a reader looking for README generation found the capability simply gone. The note states what the external skill does and the two documented ways to install it (`npx skills add`, or the Claude Code plugin marketplace), both taken from that repository's own README. It sits outside the catalog table on purpose: `tests/Installer/SkillCatalogContentTest.php` asserts one table row per skill shipped in `skills/`, and an external tool is not one of them. Per user request — *odkaz na nový generátor přidat*.

- 🗑️ **Removed**: the `readme-generator` Agent skill and every reference to it. README generation moves to the standalone [`pekral/github-readme-generator`](https://github.com/pekral/github-readme-generator) tool, which becomes the single generator for `README.md` files. Keeping a second generator inside this package would have meant two sources of truth for the same output. `skills/readme-generator/SKILL.md` is deleted, the **Meta & tooling** catalog table in `README.md` loses its `readme-generator` row, and the advertised skill count drops 54 → 53 in `README.md` (three places), `docs/installation.md`, and both launch articles under `docs/marketing/`. No test pinned the skill: `tests/Installer/SkillCatalogContentTest.php` and `tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php` derive the count from the `skills/` directory and the catalog rows, so both follow the deletion on their own. Per user request — *chci smazat skill který generuje readme.md soubor, hlavní generátor readme souborů bude github-readme-generator*.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: `paths: []` was never the reference-only scope this package documented it as, and nine rules were silently always-on because of it (#45). `tests/Pest.php` described the state as *“each reaches an agent only when a skill, an agent file, or another rule names it”*; the loader has never behaved that way. An **empty list reads exactly like no key at all**, so `code-review/general.md`, `code-review/core-analysis.md`, `code-review/review-process.md`, `code-testing/general.md`, `jira/general.md`, `php/dependency-selection.md`, `refactoring/general.md`, `reports/general.md` and `security/general.md` — roughly 220 kB, on the order of 55 000 tokens — loaded into **every** session of every consuming project, `jira/general.md` included in a repository that has never touched JIRA. The tests asserted the opposite, so an author choosing a scope for a new rule got the reverse of what the registry promised, and the reach that issues #274 and #275 spent their diffs narrowing was never actually narrowed for these files. **The measurement**, taken first-hand in a live `claude-opus-5` session and matching an independent reproduction from the day before: all nine `paths: []` rules were present in the system prompt from the opening turn, alongside the four rules that declare no key; **no** path-scoped rule was present at all; and `compound-engineering/orchestration.md`, scoped to `.claude/run/**`, was **absent at session start and attached mid-session** the moment the session read a file under `.claude/run/`. That third observation is what settles it in both directions — glob-scoping genuinely works and is dynamic, so the empty list is not a scope that matches nothing, it is always-on wearing path-scoping's spelling. **The category is retired.** The loader has two states and every rule now declares the one it actually has: `paths: []` is banned outright and a test walks the whole `rules/` tree to keep it out, while a second test pairs every rule against `ruleExtensionAlwaysOnFiles()` so a rule that omits the key without being claimed as always-on, or claims always-on while carrying a key, fails the suite. **Seven become always-on** by dropping the key, because each governs an *activity* — reviewing a diff, talking to JIRA, refactoring, publishing a report, reading external content — and no path glob names an activity. Inventing one would silence the rule exactly where it applies: that is the variant this changelog already records as rejected for `security/general.md` under #12, because glob-scoping it would mute the Untrusted Content Boundary on precisely the docs-and-tracker runs where external content arrives, and the same reasoning carries `reports/general.md`, `writing/general.md` and the code-review set. Their token cost is real and **unchanged by this fix** — it was always being paid, only undeclared — and shrinking it is a content problem, not a frontmatter one. **Two become path-scoped**, because each names a concrete file a session must touch: `code-testing/general.md` on `tests/**`, `**/tests/**`, `**/*Test.php`, and `php/dependency-selection.md` on `composer.json` / `**/composer.json`. Both fail safe when the glob misses, since the skills and rules that use them still name them via `@rules/…`. `composer.lock` is deliberately **not** a glob: that rule's own Scope section excludes version bumps of packages already present, which is exactly what a lock-only change is. **This reverses a mandate pinned by a test, deliberately and on the record.** Issue #277 chose `paths: []` to preserve the reach #274 and #275 had achieved, and the entry for it below says plainly that *“the reading is not verifiable from inside this repository, so the choice is recorded here and pinned by a test rather than assumed — and it fails safe either way”*. It did not fail safe, and the measurement above is the verification #277 could not make; #45 is the owner decision that reopens the choice, so the pin is updated rather than worked around. `ruleScopingReferenceOnlyFiles()` is deleted with the category it registered. Three places in the repository disagreed about this and now agree: `README.md` documented three settings for one key and marked nine rules `Reference`; `tests/Installer/DeploySafeSchemaChangeRuleTest.php` asserted the exact **opposite** of `tests/Pest.php`, noting that `paths: []` made its bullet *“load on every project”* — which was right about the behaviour and wrong about the mechanism. **The vendor documentation was checked, and it carries the argument further than the measurement does.** The issue also asked that the behaviour be verified against `https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory`. The implementing role holds `disallowedTools: WebSearch, WebFetch` and the *Bash capability boundary* forbids every raw-Bash outbound request, so it fabricated no citation and left the fetch to the reviewing role, which does hold those tools; the verdict recorded here is that role's reading of the page. Its *Path-specific rules* section defines exactly two states — *“These conditional rules only apply when Claude is working with files matching the specified patterns”* for a rule that carries the `paths` field, and *“Rules without a `paths` field are loaded unconditionally and apply to all files”* for one that omits it. **An empty list is defined nowhere on that page**, which otherwise covers globs, brace expansion, the 1 000-pattern budget and invalid bracket expressions. `paths: []` was therefore never a documented scope, so banning it repairs an undefined state rather than expressing a preference — the documentation settles the choice on its own, and the measurement above shows what that undefined state actually did. Closes #45

- 🐛 **Fixed**: `rules/code-review/general.md` had grown to 156 520 characters and passed the **150 000-character limit Claude Code enforces per rule file**, so the loader refused it and the **entire code-review rule set was silently inactive** in every session — the CLI reported only `⚠ .claude/rules/code-review/general.md is over the 150.0k-char limit`, while every skill that names the file kept promising a review the rules no longer backed. The file now ships as **three files** split at its own structural seams, each well under the limit: `general.md` (41 936 B) keeps the review's constraints, the project `CLAUDE.md` gate, the two-part output contract, the Exclusion Gate, and the late-iteration report scope; `core-analysis.md` (75 653 B) carries the Core Analysis walk-through, the catalog of what counts as a finding on a diff; `review-process.md` (41 569 B) carries the passes the review runs and how it reports — the diff-scoped refactoring / DRY analysis, the Validation & Coverage Gate, Critical Findings Verification (issue #537), remediation-conformance ownership, severity divergence, and the Output Rules. **No normative sentence moved**: the bytes below the frontmatter of the three files, concatenated in that order, read as the one file did but for a single line — the `Strict rule compliance` cross-reference inside the Core Analysis walk-through, which now names the file that actually carries that section instead of pointing back at `general.md`. A pointer section in `general.md` states that applying it means applying all three. Every `@rules/code-review/general.md *Section*` reference across `skills/`, `agents/`, and the other rules now names the file that carries that section. The tests that ask whether the rule set states something ask it of the whole set through a new `codeReviewRuleContents()` helper, so no future split can make an assertion pass or fail by which file a sentence landed in. Splitting the growth sink matters: the file gained ~10 kB a week (94 kB on 2026-07-13 → 156 kB on 2026-08-26), and nearly all of it landed in the Core Analysis walk-through, which now has a file and a ceiling of its own.

- ✨ **Added**: the **`github-release-roadmap`** skill (#34) — plans a release roadmap for one GitHub repository at a time and stops short of every irreversible action: it never publishes a release, pushes code, closes an issue, or deletes or archives anything. The workflow is split by a **non-negotiable approval gate**. Phase one only reads: it enumerates **all** open and closed issues with pagination exhausted, plus labels, milestones, releases, tags, and the owner's Projects, and stops before proposing an executable plan when any enumeration came back incomplete — a roadmap built on a partially-read backlog silently drops work, which is exactly the failure the gate exists to prevent. It then classifies each candidate **semantically** — requested category, change type (`bugfix` / `feature` / `breaking`), matching existing label, rationale, dependencies, confidence — treats the user's categories as release outcomes rather than as GitHub labels, and derives the next version from the selected scope. Phase two mutates GitHub only after the user confirms one explicit **confirmation package**, and every mutation re-reads its target first, reuses what already matches, and is verified by reading it back rather than by trusting an exit code — so a rerun after a partial failure detects the work that already landed instead of rolling it back destructively. Label proposals are strictly additive: an existing label whose meaning fits is reused, an existing label is never replaced or removed. Two reference files carry the detail — `references/github-cli-workflow.md` (the exact `gh` commands, the default `--limit` / pagination traps that truncate silently, and the read-back verification pass) and `references/release-policy.md` (version-source precedence, the bump ladder, and the evidence a `breaking` classification requires). The skill also documents the one thing the CLI cannot do: `gh` can create and populate a Project but has no command to create a saved view or set its layout to Roadmap, so the run either copies a template Project that already carries that view or hands the user the one-time UI step — and never claims the Roadmap view was configured while that step remains.

- ⚡ **Changed**: the project's fixers and checkers now run **once, immediately before the merge**, instead of before every push. A branch used to run the full build several times — once per implementation phase, once before the pull request opened, and once per review-fix iteration — and every one of those runs re-proved what the next one would prove again. On a larger task those repeated builds dominated the wall-clock cost of delivering the change, which is what this trades away. `@skills/resolve-issue/references/quality-gates.md` replaces *Loop gate vs. final gate* with **Gate placement — deferred to the merge boundary**: nothing runs during the branch's working life, and the gate moves to the end of the work: `@skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md` *Finalization* runs it once the review loop has converged — the loop itself runs no fixers and no checkers — and the fixes it produces land as the branch's **last commit** before the pull request is offered as ready. `@skills/merge-github-pr/SKILL.md` gains a new mandatory step 3, **Pre-merge quality gate**, as the last safety net before an irreversible action: it **accepts the recorded Finalization run** only when that run is authentic, names the exact head commit being merged, is a pass, and the tree is clean, and executes the gate itself only when it does not — a PR that never went through the review loop, or one whose head moved afterwards. The gate therefore executes once per set of bytes, and a merge still never proceeds without a green one. **The merge bar itself does not move** — issue #75's guarantee that a merge never lands with a broken project is now owned by one step instead of being spread across three, and it is unconditional: a caller's explicit *"merge anytime"* waives waiting for **CI**, never the gate, where before the deferral that waiver skipped a build the branch had already run earlier. **The fixes the gate produces land as their own commit**, never as an amend of a commit already under review, and the gate re-runs on the new head until it is green. That new commit moves the head SHA, which would otherwise stale the converged code review, so the skill states exactly one narrow exemption: a commit containing **nothing but the verbatim output of the project's fixers** carries the review forward and is recorded as having done so, while a static-analysis error resolved by hand, a failing test, a coverage gap closed with new test code, or a `rector` rewrite that changed behaviour is a real change on a reviewed diff and **blocks the merge** until the review is re-run — and the classification is read from the commit's own diff, never from a `chore(gate):` subject line an author chose. An unclear case is treated as behaviour-changing. **What this costs, stated rather than discovered later:** `@rules/git/general.md` *Every commit is green* becomes *The merged head is green; intermediate commits are not gated*, so an arbitrary commit picked out of a merged branch is no longer known to pass, `git bisect` over such a range can land on a commit that fails for unrelated reasons, and a mid-branch cherry-pick may need its own gate run. Cherry-pick **independence** — disjoint file sets, groundwork ordered before its consumers — is unaffected and still required; the range replay (`git rebase --exec`) survives as an opt-in for a branch that genuinely wants a bisectable history, and stops being mandatory before every push. Three obligations survive untouched because none of them was ever about gate placement: a test and the change that makes it pass still land in the same commit (the TDD RED state is still never committed, at **Critical** severity), a reshaped branch never inherits an earlier verdict, and the gate is still the project's own rather than a hand-picked subset. **Three mechanisms are retired as a consequence, not as separate decisions.** The always-on head-SHA push-level dedup (#212) existed only to stop three call sites re-building the same commit; the deferral removes all three, so there is no second execution left to deduplicate, and its `## Gate log` brief section goes with it. The CI-result reuse for the loop gate (#124) applied only to the per-iteration gate that no longer exists — and was already structurally unreachable in this repository, since a `pull_request` checkout resolves the merge ref and could never satisfy its staleness guard. The opt-in savings-mode build-gate cache (#119) keyed a passing build by tree hash so the *next* build in the same run could cite it; with one gate run per branch there is no next build, and the branch had already removed its only writer, so it was left with two readers and nothing to serve — its `## Build gate cache` brief section is retired with it. The one reuse that still matters, the merge accepting the Finalization run, is keyed to the head SHA and needs no cache. `security-audit` still runs fresh on every gate execution because its verdict depends on when it ran rather than on what it read. The sweep covered `resolve-issue`, `process-code-review`, `class-refactoring`, `phase-planning`, `git-workflow`, `test-driven-development`, `skill-creator`, `security-remediation-checklist`, `agents/hephaestus.md` (whose scoped validation now widens its **test selection** instead of escalating to a full build), `agents/daedalus.md`, `docs/agents.md`, `README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `rules/code-testing/general.md`, `rules/php/core-standards.md`, `rules/compound-engineering/orchestration.md`, and the root `CLAUDE.md`. A first pass claimed the sweep had reached every call site and had not — the review found eight files still describing the retired behaviour, including two that mandated the removed gate outright (`skill-creator`, `security-remediation-checklist`) and a `git-workflow` recipe that still ran the per-commit range replay between the rebase and the push. Per user request.

- 🗑️ **Removed**: the `zeus` subagent is retired and its whole backlog tier moves to `daedalus` — the roster is now `daedalus`, `hephaestus`, `athena`, `argus`, `hermes` (#26). This is a retirement with **one** successor rather than the split `apollon` got, because both of zeus's modes are the same tier and there is only one agent that can hold it: `daedalus` is the orchestrator that used to *dispatch* `zeus`, so after the merge it has nobody left to delegate that work to. That is the whole reason the change is more than a file deletion. `daedalus`'s own delegation model says *"you never run the work in your own context"*, with a single exception for step 1 (resolving the source); it now carries an explicit **second** exception, *Backlog tier — triage and decomposition, run inline*, holding both modes verbatim — `github-issue-triage` for triage, `create-issues-from-text` / `create-issue` for splitting a subject too broad for one PR — together with zeus's negative boundary, so absorbing the backlog tier does not quietly hand `daedalus` the analysis role the roster still deliberately gives nobody. The one-level Task-subagent nesting rule is what forces *inline* rather than *delegated*: a backlog agent above the orchestrator would need three nesting levels and a backlog agent beside it is the peer that just went away, so the work runs in `daedalus`'s own context and the single nesting level stays where it is needed, on `hephaestus` / `athena`. Concretely: step 3 gains a third classification branch (`Backlog-only intent`) that runs the work inline and ends the run — no dispatch, no pull request, and no working-tree write-lock, so a backlog run overlaps with a writing run exactly as an analysis-only run does; step 1's *Too broad for one PR* branch routes there instead of dispatching; every terminating-path enumeration and the *Run cleanup* call-site list name the new stop; the handoff section gains `Triage done` and `Breakdown done` in **both** `Status:` and `Result:`, because a run-mode present in only one of the two is an incomplete contract; and the frontmatter `description:` gains the triage triggers (*"triage the open issues"*, *"what should we work on next"*), without which a pure backlog request would auto-delegate nowhere at all. `daedalus`'s `Bash boundary` gains exactly what zeus had and nothing more — `gh label` / `gh issue create`, reachable only through those three skills and never as a bare command it composes itself — and it still holds no `Write` / `Edit` tool, so the new writes land on the tracker, never in the working tree. The sweep covered the whole tree rather than `agents/` alone: the `Role:` dictionary and the per-role read filter drop the value, the two **L1** consent rows are re-keyed from `zeus` to `daedalus` at the same level (the actor changed, the consent did not), `docs/agents.md` loses the agent card and the naming-convention row and gains a *Retired agents* row plus a rewritten *Subagents of an agent* rationale and pipeline diagram, `README.md` loses the role card and counts five agents instead of six, `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/feature_request.yml` drops the name from its agent list, the avatar `assets/agents/zeus.svg` is deleted with its last `<img>` reference, and `assets/social-preview.svg` loses the chip with the remaining five re-centred on x=640 and re-rendered at 1280×640 (both digests updated) — that asset is derived from `agents/*.md` at test time, which is what catches a stale name inside it; the rendered `.png` beside it is the surface a grep for the removed name could never have reached. The unpublished launch-article drafts are corrected too, since `published: false` copy describing today's roster is not a historical snapshot. As with every retirement, the installer copies `agents/` but only *removes* a file the source stopped shipping when it is run with `--prune` — a project that upgrades without that flag keeps a stale, still-dispatchable `.claude/agents/zeus.md` pointing at a pipeline position nothing dispatches any more, so **run `vendor/bin/ai-olympus install --prune` after upgrading**, or delete the file by hand. The historical changelog entry that introduced `zeus` is left verbatim: it is the record of what happened, and `docs/agents.md` *Retired agents* is the canonical place a stale `@zeus` reference resolves.

- ✨ **Added**: the **`simplification-audit`** skill (#9) — a read-only, audit-only workflow that finds materially useful simplifications in a codebase's data structures, state representation, control flow, algorithms, and ownership. It runs **only** on an explicit user request for a codebase audit or for refactoring a part of the codebase; it is deliberately wired into no agent and no other skill, and a test holds that absence so a later edit cannot turn an unrelated run into a whole-repository sweep. The coordinator establishes a subsystem inventory as the coverage contract (stable ID, exact ownership boundary, key files, interfaces / call sites / tests, and a `queued` / `in review` / `recommend` / `skip` status), dispatches one bounded read-only worker per subsystem with the brief in `references/worker-brief.md`, independently verifies every returned finding against the real current bytes before accepting it, and closes with an audit-the-audit pass over coverage, duplication, materiality, schema completeness, and dependency-aware priority. Each accepted recommendation carries all eight required fields (verdict, evidence, current complexity, proposed representation, smallest implementation scope, regression risks, validation required, confidence). The skill never edits a file, runs a test, commits, pushes, or publishes anything — implementing an accepted recommendation stays with `@skills/class-refactoring/SKILL.md` and `@skills/refactor-entry-point-to-action/SKILL.md` in a separate, separately requested run.

- ✨ **Added**: a central **Untrusted Content Boundary** security rule (#12). `rules/security/general.md` states the one invariant the package applied in pieces and never wrote down: instructions from trusted context — system instructions, the user's own instructions, the current skill's or agent's own definition, orchestrator rules, and repository rules the workflow loaded as configuration — take precedence over instructions found inside external or retrieved content. External content stays **data**: a run may analyze it, quote it, summarize it, and decide from it, and it may never change an agent's role or permissions, rewrite the workflow, widen the scope, start an operation only because the text asks for it, or bypass a security rule. The rule names the trusted and untrusted sources, states that the **source** decides whether text is an instruction and never its imperative wording, lists the prompt-injection phrasings to read as suspicious, separates a trusted tool from the untrusted content that tool returns, and fixes the escalation shape — do not execute the sentence, continue the legitimate part of the task, record the attempt in the handoff, alert the orchestrator.
