Execution mechanics live in the new `skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md` *Deferred-item follow-up issues* step (dedup search → `gh issue create` / `acli` / Bugsnag-linked repo → loader re-read → cross-link, with the optional `Resolve_by_AI` backlog label when the repo uses one); `skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md` files deferred (not rejected) reviewer points and deferred pre-existing follow-ups itself instead of leaving it to the reviewer, and `skills/create-missing-tests-in-pr/SKILL.md` routes its deferred follow-ups through the same rule. Pinned by a content test in `tests/Installer/CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php`. Per user request — *pokud AI agent vědomě odloží bod z řešeného tasku, musí ho zaevidovat jako nový tracker issue, aby se na jeho řešení nezapomnělo*.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: `skills/code-review-github/scripts/load-issue.sh` no longer collapses `isDraft: false` to `null` in the emitted PR JSON — jq's `//` alternative operator treats `false` as empty, so the `$p.isDraft // null` projection made a non-draft PR indistinguishable from a missing field for consumers such as `merge-github-pr` (which reads `isDraft` off this JSON as its Draft merge gate). The projection now passes the boolean through untouched; a content regression test pins the corrected jq fragment. Pre-existing bug surfaced during the PR #22 review loop.

- 📝 **Changed**: the GitHub loader scripts now **require the full GitHub URL** — bare issue/PR numbers are rejected. `skills/code-review-github/scripts/load-issue.sh` exits 1 on a bare number (`1766`, `#1766`) with an error pointing at the full-URL form (`https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/issues/1766`): a bare number resolved the repo from the caller's cwd git remote, which silently loaded the wrong repo when the script ran outside the target checkout, and a `#`-prefixed argument never reached the script at all (the shell drops it as a comment). The now-unused `resolve_repo_from_git` helper and the unreachable issue-vs-pr discriminator branch were removed; `gather-issue-context.sh`, `parse-comments.sh`, and `download-attachments.sh` inherit the URL-only contract via delegation and document it. Every skill referencing the GitHub loader chain (`resolve-issue` + `references/source-detection.md`, `prepare-issue-context`, `analyze-problem`, `code-review-github`, `process-code-review`, `merge-github-pr`, `pr-summary`, `tester-cookbook`, `assignment-compliance-check`) now instructs `<URL>` instead of `<NUMBER|URL>` and, where the assignment may carry only `#123`, tells the agent to build the full URL from the current repo's `origin` remote first. Installer content tests updated to pin the new usage strings and the rejection message. Per user request — *helper skript load-issue.sh vždy musí dostat plnou GitHub URL, nikdy jen #1766 — s holým číslem selže*.

- 📝 **Changed**: folded the `github/awesome-copilot` **create-readme** conventions into the existing **`readme-generator`** skill instead of adding a duplicate `create-readme` skill (#11). `skills/readme-generator/SKILL.md` now instructs the README author to: lead the header with a discovered project logo / icon; use GitHub Flavored Markdown with GitHub admonitions (`> [!NOTE]`, `> [!IMPORTANT]`, `> [!WARNING]`) where they add clarity; keep the prose concise and restrained on emoji; **not** embed `LICENSE` / `CONTRIBUTING` / `CHANGELOG` / `SECURITY` as full sections (link to the dedicated root files instead); and take structural / tonal inspiration from four well-crafted open-source reference READMEs. The skill count is unchanged (no new skill added). Per user request — *rozšířit readme-generator místo duplicitního create-readme skillu*.

- 🎨 **Changed**: refreshed the project logo (#12) — `assets/logo.png` now shows the tech-stack grid (PHP, Laravel, Livewire, Alpine.js, Filament, Tailwind CSS, Vite, Playwright, MySQL, PostgreSQL, Redis, Docker). The filename is unchanged, so the README header reference (`<img src="assets/logo.png">`) needs no edit. Per user request — *aktualizace loga v readme.md, název souboru ponechán*.

- 📝 **Changed**: README cleanup and a more human-readable subagent roster (#10). The quick-start install command now uses `vendor/bin/ai-olympus install --editor=cursor --force`; the **Installing rules from GitHub (Cursor only)** section and the whole **🎯 Skills Overview** per-category catalog were removed; and the **Claude Code Subagents** section was rewritten from a cramped three-column table into per-agent cards, each showing the agent's avatar from `assets/agents/`. Those seven avatars were downscaled from 1254×1254 (~2.3 MB each) to 256×256 (~120 KB each) — roughly a 95% size cut — to speed up page loads (`docs/agents.md` renders them at `width="48"`, so the smaller source is unaffected). Dangling references reconciled: `skills/skill-creator/SKILL.md` no longer tells authors to bump a "Skills Overview" header or add a catalog-table row (only the "Why This Package" count remains), `docs/agents.md` now says to add a new agent to the README roster card instead of the removed table, and `tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php` drops the assertions on the removed catalog strings while still pinning the advertised skill count. The README was also reformatted following the `github/awesome-copilot` **create-readme** skill conventions: bold-prefix callouts (`**Important:**`, the `talos` troubleshooting note) became GitHub admonitions (`> [!IMPORTANT]`, `> [!NOTE]`), and the `## License` section was dropped in favour of the dedicated `LICENSE` file (the header license badge still links to it). Per user request — *úprava readme.md dle issue #10 a formátování dle create-readme skillu*.

- 🗑️ **Removed**: 13 unused Agent skills and every dangling reference to them (#6) — `article-writing`, `automation-audit-ops`, `autonomous-loops`, `autoresolve-oldest-github-issue`, `benchmark-optimization-loop`, `benchmark`, `blueprint`, `composer-update`, `penetration-tester`, `production-audit`, `refresh-claude-md`, `slack-messaging`, `test-like-human`. README skill count bumped 62 → 49; the **Content & Writing** table lost its `article-writing` row (only `readme-generator` remains) and the *performance benchmarking* mention was dropped from the Skills Overview intro. Cross-cutting cleanup: `agents/hermes.md` no longer delegates release-note / marketing-summary authoring to `article-writing` — the no-hollow-AI-phrasing contract is now inlined directly in the agent; `agents/apollon.md` drops the "hunt broken flows" step that ran `test-like-human` (description, orchestrated-skills list, and handoff `Broken flows` field removed accordingly); `agents/daedalus.md` inlines the oldest-open-issue selection convention (label `Resolve_by_AI`, excluding already-claimed issues) instead of delegating to `autoresolve-oldest-github-issue`. `rules/compound-engineering/general.mdc`, `rules/git/general.mdc`, and `rules/php/dependency-selection.mdc` drop their references to the removed skills; `skills/product-capability/SKILL.md` no longer hands off to the removed `blueprint`; the `test-like-human` "After Completion" / "run it if testable" bullets are removed from `skills/code-review`, `skills/code-review-jira`, `skills/code-review-bugsnag`, `skills/process-code-review`, `skills/resolve-issue`, `skills/create-missing-tests-in-pr`, `skills/laravel-telescope`, `skills/e2e-testing`, `skills/skill-creator`, and `skills/tester-cookbook`. `.github/workflows/composer-update.yml` is unchanged — it is a plain CI job that runs `composer update` directly and never invoked the deleted skill. Installer content tests (`tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php`, `AgentsTest.php`, `CodeReviewContentTest.php`, `ReportsContentTest.php`, `CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php`) had their now-obsolete pinned assertions removed, and a `not->toContain('test-like-human')` regression guard was added for the CR/resolution skills. Per user request — *odstranit tyto skilly a aktualizovat CLAUDE.md a README.md podle aktuálního codebase*.

- 📝 **Changed**: the read-only **code-review agents (`argos`, `athena`) may now opt into an isolated git worktree** to run their parallel CR pass when isolation is genuinely needed, and **`daedalus` cleans every CR worktree up** after the run / merge so the repository and codebase stay clean. This refines the earlier *git worktrees are forbidden across the whole workflow* policy: the **writing path (`talos`) still never uses worktrees** and concurrent writers still serialise on the single shared working tree via the write-lock — only the read-only CR pass gains the explicit-request opt-in of `@rules/git/general.mdc` *Worktrees / Workspaces*, which carries no write-lock and never contends with the writing path. Each CR agent creates the worktree with `git worktree add` (a throwaway read-only review checkout — never edits / commits / pushes there), records its path in the handoff, and either hands it to `daedalus` for removal in cleanup (step 7: `git worktree remove` + `git worktree prune`, no `--force`) or removes it itself when run standalone. Documented in `agents/daedalus.md` *Concurrency & the working-tree write-lock* (now *Worktrees — writing path stays on the shared tree, read-only CR may isolate*) + step 6/7, `agents/argos.md` / `agents/athena.md` *Parallel review worktree*, and mirrored in `docs/agents.md`. Per user request — *CR agenti smí používat git worktrees pro paralelní code review; Daedalus po dokončení a merge vše uklidí, aby v repository nebyl bordel*.

- ✨ **Added**: new **`laravel-authorization-review`** skill (#716) — a Laravel-native authorization / IDOR (broken object-level authorization, BOLA) reviewer for use in Laravel code review. Adapted from the open-source `laravel-authorization-review` skill, it walks the authorization chain of every HTTP route (middleware → `authorize`/policy/gate → Eloquent query scoping → API Resource output), anchors **every** finding to real `php artisan route:list --json` output plus a cited `file:line`, classifies by confidence (High / Medium / Low), maps each finding onto the repo CR severity scale (Critical / Moderate / Minor) per `@rules/code-review/general.mdc`, and produces a per-route coverage map. Read-only / advise-only — never edits code. Ships `references/auth-patterns.md` (how Laravel authz legitimately appears across middleware, policies, gates, and `route:list --json`), `references/public-by-design.md` (routes meant to be unauthenticated), and `templates/report.md` (coverage-map report template). Complements `laravel-security` (broad 7-area audit) and `security-review` by going deep on the authorization lane. Catalogued under `skills/laravel-authorization-review/`; README skill count bumped 61 → 62.

- 📝 **Changed**: `daedalus` now processes **every task it resolves from the issue tracker strictly sequentially — one source at a time, never fanning out in parallel** — and **git worktrees are forbidden** across the whole workflow. The former *Parallel analysis fan-out* (multiple `metis` runs in one message) is replaced by *Sequential processing of multiple sources*: a single request that resolves to several issues finishes one source's run completely before starting the next, ordered by the dependency-aware plan (or oldest-first). The concurrency / write-lock section drops every isolated-worktree escape — concurrent full-delivery runs serialise on the single shared working tree, and a run that finds a live lock holder waits and retries rather than spinning up a worktree. Documented in `agents/daedalus.md` *Concurrency & the working-tree write-lock* + *Sequential processing of multiple sources* and mirrored in `docs/agents.md`. The intra-task `argos` ‖ `athena` security/quality CR pass stays parallel — it is two reviews of one task, not two tasks. Per user request — *všechny úkoly z issue trackeru řešit systematicky a nikoliv paralelně; výslovně zakázat git worktrees*.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: parallel agents (`argos` ‖ `athena` in the step-6 CR pass) now reliably **share their split output through the per-issue shared brief**. Two gaps are closed: (1) two concurrent `cat >> "$BRIEF"` appends could interleave and corrupt the brief — every append is now guarded by a short **per-brief append lock** (`mkdir "$BRIEF.lock"` / `rmdir`, keyed to the source slug, held only for the write, with a ~10 s stale-reclaim so a crashed holder never deadlocks the peer — mirroring the run write-lock) so parallel handoffs serialise for the write while staying concurrent otherwise; and (2) a parallel sibling's handoff is **not** visible in the brief read taken at the start of a peer's run, so the docs no longer claim `argos` reads `athena`'s findings mid-run — a new **barrier** rule makes `daedalus` wait for every parallel handoff to land in the brief, then the consolidating agent (`argos`) re-reads the **complete** brief (re-dispatched if it finished first) before publishing and driving the fix loop. Documented once in `agents/daedalus.md` *Shared task brief* → *Parallel handoff sharing* and referenced from `agents/argos.md` / `agents/athena.md`. Per user request — *když běží dva agenti paralelně (např. při CR), jejich výstup musí být k dispozici ostatním přes per-issue lokální paměť*.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: AI agents no longer write **commit messages / PR titles in the assignment language** — the language-propagation prose told every specialist to "reply in Czech" but the English-always carve-out listed only branch names, so an agent following it produced a Czech commit. The carve-out in all seven agents (`daedalus`, `talos`, `argos`, `athena`, `metis`, `apollon`, `hermes`) now explicitly keeps **commit messages and PR titles in English** per `@rules/git/general.mdc` *Commit Messages*, regardless of the assignment language. Per user request — *AI agent vytváří commit message v češtině, má být vždy anglicky*.

## [0.9.1] - 2026-06-22

- ✨ **Added**: **Laravel Security Audit workflow (7 areas)** for the `athena` security agent (#668) — Authorization/IDOR-BOLA, Authentication, Validation, XSS, File upload, Secrets/config, and Dependencies, each with a per-finding regression-test sketch and a defensive (authorized-auditor) framing, mapped onto the repo's 3-level CR convergence scale. Added under `skills/laravel-security/` and referenced from `agents/athena.md`.

- ✨ **Added**: **Malicious File Upload Content** security rule covering the CONTENT/RENDER layer of uploads (#680) — stored XSS from file content, SVG with active content, CSV/Excel formula injection, HTML/JS in filename and metadata, polyglots, and missing nosniff/Content-Disposition. Added to `@rules/security/backend.md` (canonical) plus the `frontend.md` / `mobile.md` mirrors, with review walks in `@skills/code-review/SKILL.md` and `@skills/security-review/SKILL.md` and an inert malicious-upload payload dataset (`skills/security-review/datasets/malicious-uploads/`, 6 categories) for writing tests. Deduped against the existing TYPE/TRANSPORT upload rule so each violation raises exactly one finding.

- ✨ **Added**: new **Variable Ordering & Lazy Evaluation** code-review category (#681) — flags an expensive operation (DB query / HTTP call / collection materialization / heavy computation) bound to a variable before it is needed on a path (early-return / guard / `continue` / `break`) where the value is never used, steering toward just-in-time evaluation near first use. Lives in `@rules/code-review/general.mdc` + `@skills/code-review/SKILL.md` with severity Minor (default) / Moderate (hot path, loop, per-request entry point), an IS-NOT boundary, and a gating clause; CR wrappers inherit automatically.

- 📝 **Changed**: bug-fix resolution now **mandates strict TDD — a failing test first** (#682). `@skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md` `### If bug` rewrites steps 8–10 to RED (write the reproducing failing test) → **verify the test fails for the right reason (blocking)** → GREEN (fix), driven by `@skills/test-driven-development/SKILL.md`.

- 📝 **Changed**: code-review now flags **caching of objects** as a **Moderate** finding (#683) — `@skills/code-review/SKILL.md` gains a detection bullet that reports any cache write storing an object (Eloquent model, DTO, Collection of models, serialized object); only scalar / array / non-object payloads stay allowed. Carries the mandatory reproduction fields and a gating clause symmetric with the existing inline-Eloquent rule so no severity collision is raised; inherited automatically by `code-review-github` / `-jira` / `-bugsnag`.

- 📝 **Changed**: code-review Coverage gate now **reuses CI results when available** instead of re-running every checker locally. `@skills/code-review/SKILL.md` Validation → Coverage gate gains a **Reuse CI results** rule: before running any local check, the CR reads `statusCheckRollup[]` from the PR JSON and — when needed — fetches the run log via `gh run view` to determine which checks already ran on the exact PR head commit; green checks are reused directly, and only missing or non-green checks are executed locally. The most common case is that CI already ran the full test suite but did not publish a changed-files coverage report, so the CR runs only the coverage tooling. A staleness guard ensures CI results are accepted only when the run's head SHA matches the current PR `headRefOid`; failing / in-progress checks are always treated as missing. All three CR wrappers (`code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`, `code-review-bugsnag`) gain a **CI coverage of checks** step in Pre-checks that extracts the check map from the loaded PR JSON and forwards it to the Coverage gate. The 100% changed-files coverage requirement stays mandatory — only the source (CI vs local) changes. Per user request — *uprav skilly pro code review tak, že když je při CR k dispozici výstup z CI a v něm už proběhly testy, CR je nemá spouštět znovu — lokálně se spustí jen to, co v CI chybí*.

- 📝 **Changed**: JIRA CR comments are now **always-new per CR run**, consistent with GitHub. `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/upsert-comment.sh` previously searched for a prior comment by the same acli actor and edited it in place; the script now always calls `acli jira workitem comment create` so every CR iteration appends a fresh comment at the bottom of the JIRA thread — visible without scrolling through edit history. The hidden `{anchor:cr-comment-actor-<slug>}` marker is still appended to the body for traceability but no longer drives any lookup or update. SKILL.md wording updated across `code-review-jira`, `code-review`, `pr-summary`, and `process-code-review` to reflect the always-new convention. Per user request — *skilly pro report-summary do issue trackerů mají při každé iteraci CR founded NOVÝ komentář, ne editovat jeden v místě*.

- 📝 **Changed**: code-review skills now **always require checking out the change branch** and reviewing against the actual working tree, not a remote diff in isolation. The soft "ensure you are on the branch" / "switch to the PR branch" line in `@skills/code-review/SKILL.md`, `@skills/code-review-github/SKILL.md`, `@skills/code-review-jira/SKILL.md`, and `@skills/code-review-bugsnag/SKILL.md` is replaced by a **mandatory Branch checkout gate**: before any analysis step the review runs `git fetch` + `git checkout <branch>` + `git pull`, confirms local `HEAD` matches the PR head SHA, and **stops and reports** instead of reviewing from the diff when the checkout fails (missing ref, detached `HEAD`, or local changes that would be overwritten). The read-only constraint is tightened in lockstep — checking out the branch is now **required** (not merely "allowed") while working-tree mutation and pushing stay forbidden. Per user request — *uprav skilly pro code review tak, aby vyžadovali vždy přepnutí na větev se změnami a CR se dělal nad aktuálním codebase*.

- ✨ **Added**: `SECURITY.md` at the repository root (#664) — documents the plugin trust model (`allow-plugins`, the standard Composer opt-in), the two security-sensitive installer flags (`--allow-bundled-scripts` and `--allow-subagent-writes`) with their exact behaviour, safety guarantees, and implementation references (`src/InstallerClaudeSettings.php`), a table of every path the installer writes, and a responsible-disclosure channel (GitHub Security Advisory preferred). Cross-linked with `README.md` (CLI Switches), `docs/agents.md` (Troubleshooting — subagent file writes blocked), and `docs/plans/agent-sandbox-write-blocked.md`.

- ✨ **Added**: public **editor compatibility matrix** in `README.md` documenting support status across editors — Cursor ✅ (rules + skills), Claude ✅ (rules + skills + agents), Codex skills ✅ / rules experimental (unverified until confirmed) (#663).

- 📝 **Changed**: README and `composer.json` package metadata reposition the project explicitly as a PHP/Laravel Composer plugin (installs Cursor/Claude/Codex rules + agent skills), not a generic AI-rules bundle (#662).

- 🗑️ **Removed**: bundled launcher `ai-olympus-resolve-loop.sh` and its Composer `bin` registration — no longer needed. The script, the README *Autonomous issue loop* section, and the `agents/daedalus.md` / `docs/agents.md` references to it are dropped. Consumers that invoked `vendor/bin/ai-olympus-resolve-loop.sh` should drive the loop directly via `claude --permission-mode auto "/loop …"` instead.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: JIRA `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/load-issue.sh` now correctly unwraps a Java/Groovy `toString` custom field whose `json={…}` payload is followed by trailing content (the outer map's closing brace). `tryParseTrimEnd` fed an invalid candidate to `fromjson?`, whose suppressed error yields `empty` in jq, and `.parsed = (empty)` collapsed the whole `until` iteration to `empty` — so the fallback returned `null` on the first non-parsing trim instead of trimming down to the valid JSON prefix. As a result `devSummary` (PR / branch / commit counts from the dev panel) silently degraded to `null` for every such field. Coalescing `fromjson?` to `null` keeps the iteration alive until it trims to a parseable prefix.

## [0.9.0] - 2026-06-20

- ✨ **Added**: per-project **compound memory** mechanism (#626) — operationalizes the previously aspirational *Compound Memory* section of `@rules/compound-engineering/general.mdc`. The rule now defines a concrete memory file convention (`docs/memory/PROJECT_MEMORY.md` in the project being worked on, separate from the hand-maintained `CLAUDE.md`), a greppable one-lesson-per-entry format, a three-gate **promotion bar** (recurs beyond the task / cost real effort / not already captured) so only the most important lessons are stored, a per-write **curation pass** (dedup / supersede / prune / soft-cap), the read protocol, and the secrets/PII prohibition. A new write-side skill **`record-project-memory`** runs at task convergence to distil and append surviving lessons. The read side is hooked into `daedalus` (gather phase folds matching entries into the shared brief's new `## Project memory` section), `analyze-problem` (*Context extraction*), and `prepare-issue-context`; the write side is hooked into `resolve-issue` (*Final report*), `process-code-review` (*Completion*), and the `daedalus` final-report step (before brief cleanup). The file lives in the target project, never in this shared rules package, and the installer never touches it (project data, not package data), so it survives re-installation without an installer change. Also fixes a pre-existing dangling cross-reference: `daedalus` and `talos` referenced a *Blocked delegation is a hard stop* section that did not exist in the rule — it is now present.

- ✨ **Added**: opt-in installer flag `--allow-subagent-writes` that unblocks dispatched-subagent file writes (`talos`) by prepending two scoped permission entries — `Edit(//<project>/**)` and `Write(//<project>/**)` — to `permissions.allow` in the project's `.claude/settings.local.json` (effective with `--editor=claude` / `--editor=all`). A dispatched subagent runs non-interactively, so a write is denied unless the path is pre-allowed; the scoped entries grant exactly that, permanently and across headless runs. The flag is **opt-in by design** (mirrors `--allow-bundled-scripts`) — the package grants nothing by default — is **idempotent** (leaves existing allow entries untouched, adds only a missing entry), and validates the result via a new public `InstallerClaudeSettings::validateSubagentWritePermissions()` before and after the write (round-trip) so a malformed `settings.local.json` can never be produced. `settings.local.json` (personal, git-ignored) is the target because the entries carry a machine-absolute path. `docs/agents.md` *Troubleshooting — subagent file writes blocked*, `README.md`, and `docs/plans/agent-sandbox-write-blocked.md` document the remediation and flag. Supersedes the initially-shipped sandbox-block approach, which did not unblock subagent writes in practice (verified in a real project). Per user request — *funkční je Možnost A: scoped Edit/Write v permissions.allow v settings.local.json; oprav installer aby `--allow-subagent-writes` generoval tohle*.
- 📝 **Changed**: introducing a **new static-analysis / linter suppression** is now a **Moderate** code-review finding — suppressions should be fixed, not created. `@rules/php/core-standards.mdc` PHP Practices gains *Do not introduce new static-analysis / linter suppressions*, covering PHPCS (`// phpcs:ignore` / `phpcs:disable` / `@phpcsSuppress` / `@codingStandardsIgnore*`), PHPStan (`@phpstan-ignore` / `@phpstan-ignore-next-line` / new `ignoreErrors` / baseline additions), Psalm / Phan (`@psalm-suppress` / `@phan-suppress` / baseline), PHPMD `@SuppressWarnings(...)`, and the PHP `@` operator. `@skills/code-review/SKILL.md` adds a Core Analysis bullet that flags each new suppression on a changed line as **Moderate** with a fix-the-root-cause Suggested Fix. The narrow exemption: a suppression that is **both** narrowly scoped (specific rule / error identifier, single line) **and** documents inline why it is an unfixable third-party / framework false positive; `assert($var !== null)` for a required-unused variable stays the sanctioned `UnusedVariable` fix (a real assertion, not a suppression). Per user request — *pokud je v kódu nový typ ignorace (např. `@phpcsSuppress` či pro phpstan), označit to v CR jako Moderate; tyto chyby se mají řešit, ne vytvářet*.

- 📝 **Changed**: stack-agnostic skills now load `@rules/php/core-standards.mdc` **conditionally**, not on every run. The five language-neutral skills — `analyze-problem`, `readme-generator`, `skill-creator`, `smartest-project-addition`, `understand-propose-implement-verify` — apply the PHP standards **only once it is established that the project is a PHP project and the work touches PHP code**, and skip them for non-PHP work (docs, tooling, infra, markdown, config). PHP/Laravel-centric skills (code-review, security-review, class-refactoring, the test skills, etc.) keep applying the rule unconditionally because they only ever run on PHP code. This stops a non-PHP analysis (e.g. an agent/sandbox or docs problem) from reading the 135-line PHP standards it does not need. Per user request — *skill analyze-problem volá vždy „Read rules/php/core-standards.mdc"; tato pravidla číst až poté, co je jisté, že jde o PHP projekt a změny v PHP*.
- 📝 **Changed**: a write-capable agent blocked by the harness sandbox / permission layer is now a **hard stop, never a silent bypass**. `@rules/compound-engineering/general.mdc` gains *Blocked delegation is a hard stop* — when a dispatched implementer (`talos`) has its `Write` / `Edit` refused at runtime, the run reports the blocker with its remediation and **never** silently finishes the work in the main thread (which would bypass the implementer→reviewer loop and quality gates). `agents/talos.md` adds a `Blocked: sandbox denied file write` terminal handoff; `agents/daedalus.md` escalates it instead of implementing the change itself; `docs/agents.md` and `README.md` add a *Troubleshooting — subagent file writes blocked* section explaining that file writes are an environment grant (subagents run non-interactively) and how the human enables them (`--permission-mode acceptEdits`, `permissions.allow`, or sandbox working-tree access). The package never flips these security-sensitive settings automatically. Plan artifact: `docs/plans/agent-sandbox-write-blocked.md`. Per user report — *subagenti daedalus/talos narazili na sandbox blokující zápis souborů, takže jsem implementaci dokončil přímo v hlavním vlákně*.
- 📝 **Changed**: public methods must now return typed **DTOs instead of raw associative arrays**. `@rules/php/core-standards.mdc` Structure gains a return-value counterpart to the >4-parameter rule — when a public method produces a structured set of values (a record, a multi-key payload, an array shape with named keys) it returns a `final readonly` DTO, not an `array`; `@rules/laravel/architecture.mdc` DTOs adds *Public methods return typed DTOs, not associative arrays*, tightens the Data Builder return contract (DTO by default, normalized array only for a framework/vendor write-payload contract), and lists the violation under CR Severity *moderate*; `@skills/code-review/SKILL.md` enforces it as a **Moderate** finding on every diff. Exemptions (cited explicitly where enforced): single scalar / `bool` / `void` returns, a homogeneous list or `Collection` of one type, and array shapes fixed by a framework / vendor contract (`toArray()`, `jsonSerialize()`, `Arrayable`, `casts()`, FormRequest `rules()`, overrides of a `vendor/`-owned signature). Per user request — *uprav skilly a pravidla tak, aby public metody vracely místo pole DTO objekty*.
- 📝 **Changed**: agents no longer create git **worktrees / workspaces automatically**. `@rules/git/general.mdc` gains a *Worktrees / Workspaces* section: by default an agent works in the current branch and working tree and never runs `git worktree add` on its own initiative — an isolated worktree is created **only on explicit request** (or for a parallel multi-unit workflow the user explicitly opted into) and is removed when the unit is done. `@skills/autonomous-loops/SKILL.md` is aligned so the per-unit worktree is an opt-in property of parallel orchestration, not a default of a single sequential loop. Per user request — *uprav pravidla pro agenty tak, že automaticky nebudou vytvářet git workspaces, ale jen na vyžádání*.
- 📝 **Changed**: the `daedalus` orchestrator now **stops at analysis when only analysis is requested**. A new scope-classification step distinguishes *analysis-only intent* (analyse / diagnose / scope / plan / prepare an assignment, with no implementation request) from *full-delivery intent*: on analysis-only it dispatches `metis`, returns the published plan-artifact / assignment link as the deliverable, and **stops** — it does not dispatch `talos` or implement anything, so a later run (or a human) can pick up implementation when they choose. The handoff supports an `Analysis done` terminal status and a `metis` (analysis-only) route. Per user request — *uprav agenty tak, že když je vyžádána pouze analýza, tak se automaticky neprogramuje, ale vytvoří se zadání či analýza pro další zpracování*.
- 📝 **Changed**: the `argos` code-review agent now selects the review skill from the inserted source. When the input carries a tracker URL/reference the matching wrapper runs (GitHub → `@skills/code-review-github/SKILL.md`, JIRA → `@skills/code-review-jira/SKILL.md`, Bugsnag → `@skills/code-review-bugsnag/SKILL.md`); when no source can be resolved (no tracker URL/reference and the current branch maps to no PR/tracker item) argos falls back to the default read-only `@skills/code-review/SKILL.md`, reviewing the local working-tree / branch diff and returning the findings in its handoff instead of publishing to a PR. This overrides the "ask the user" note in `@skills/resolve-issue/references/source-detection.md` for the review path only — the shared detection table is unchanged. A lock-in test in `tests/InstallerTest.php` fixes the fallback contract in place. Per user request — *uprav rozhodující logiku tak, aby se podle vloženého zdroje (URL) vybral příslušný code-review skill, a když URL není dostupná, použij výchozí `code-review`*.
- ✨ **Added**: new `readme-generator` Agent skill (#599), adapted from the [VoltAgent awesome-claude-code-subagents](https://github.com/VoltAgent/awesome-claude-code-subagents/blob/main/categories/06-developer-experience/readme-generator.md) reference and rewritten for this package's skill format and Claude Code tooling. The skill generates or rewrites a maintainer-ready `README.md` (and sibling root docs such as `CONTRIBUTING` / `SECURITY`) from the project's actual code, manifests, scripts, and tests — a **zero-hallucination** scan (identity & audience → deep repository scan → capture real commands → extract real examples → research only to fill gaps → draft → validate → optional stage/commit) that extracts copy-paste-correct install / usage / configuration values and flags anything the repository cannot prove as a maintainer `TODO` instead of inventing it. It writes documentation only (never touches production code, tests, or config) and runs git staging / commit / push **only on explicit user request**. README skill count bumped 58 → 59. Per issue #599 — *chci vytvořit nebo aktualizovat skilly pro generování readme.md souborů z VoltAgent readme-generator*.
- ✨ **Added**: new `penetration-tester` Agent skill (#597), adapted from the [VoltAgent awesome-claude-code-subagents](https://github.com/VoltAgent/awesome-claude-code-subagents/blob/main/categories/04-quality-security/penetration-tester.md) reference and rewritten for this package's PHP / Laravel / Filament / Livewire / Blade stack and Claude Code tooling. The skill runs an authorized, methodology-driven offensive assessment — a blocking pre-engagement gate (explicit request + authorization + scope + rules of engagement + success criteria) followed by ordered phases (reconnaissance → vulnerability identification → safe proof-of-concept exploit validation → impact / risk assessment → remediation / reporting) — and delivers a risk-rated report with an engagement summary, per-finding PoCs, and a remediation roadmap. It is **read-only / non-destructive** (never modifies, stages, commits, or pushes code; never runs a destructive, persistent, or service-disrupting payload; redacts secrets / tokens / PII) and **gated to the explicit request only** — it does not run on a normal `code-review`, `security-review`, or `resolve-issue` pass, and refuses when authorization or scope is missing. Cross-referenced against its neighbors (`/security-review/SKILL.md`, `/security-bounty-hunter/SKILL.md`, `/security-threat-analysis/SKILL.md`) so the boundaries stay distinct. README skill count bumped 57 → 58. Per issue #597 — *připrav nový skill podle VoltAgent penetration-tester; tento skill se spustí pouze pokud zažádám o penetrační testy*.
- 📝 **Changed**: non-default branches must be rebased onto the latest default branch when they are pulled, keeping every side branch current with the default branch. `@rules/git/general.mdc` gains a **Pull Policy** section: the default branch (resolved by name — `main` on some repos, `master` on others, never hardcoded as `origin/main`) is pulled directly, but any feature / fix / PR branch is synced in a fixed order — `git fetch origin`, `git pull --rebase` to take the branch's own remote first, then `git rebase "origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH"` to bring the default branch in, resolve conflicts, and `git push --force-with-lease` (no trailing `git pull`, which would replay the commits onto the old remote tip and undo the sync); when that rebase changes `composer.lock`, `composer install` runs immediately so the installed packages match the new lockfile. Read-only review skills are explicitly exempt (they `git pull` only to read the diff and never rewrite the branch). The mechanics are documented in `@skills/git-workflow/SKILL.md` (a new *Pull policy: sync a side branch before pulling it* section plus a Done-when bullet), and `@skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md` now applies the policy on the PR branch it owns before processing review feedback. Per user request — *před každým pull vedlejší větve udělej rebase hlavní větve do ní, a pokud to přinese změny, rovnou spusť composer install*.
- ✨ **Added**: 8 new Agent skills adapted from the [ECC](https://github.com/affaan-m/ECC) skill collection, each rewritten for this package's PHP 8.4 / Laravel / Filament / Livewire / Alpine / Blade / Pest / MySQL stack and Claude Code tooling, and validated at `composer skill-check` 100/100 (#571). **Planning & delivery:** `product-capability` (turn an underspecified PRD into an engineering-ready capability plan exposing invariants, interfaces, and unresolved decisions, then hand off to `blueprint` or `@skills/create-issues-from-text/SKILL.md`), `blueprint` (decompose a large objective into 3–12 one-PR steps with dependency edges, cold-start context briefs, and exit criteria, reviewed adversarially and registered as Markdown), and `autonomous-loops` (reference catalog of autonomous loop patterns from a single sequential pipeline to multi-agent DAG orchestration, anchored to `@skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md`, `@skills/autoresolve-oldest-github-issue/SKILL.md`, `@skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md`, `@skills/merge-github-pr/SKILL.md`, the built-in `/loop`, and `composer build` / `composer skill-check` as the gate between iterations). **Code review & architecture:** `production-audit` (read-only, local-evidence-only production-readiness audit across auth, data integrity, payments, jobs, and deployment lenses, returning a scored ship/block verdict with hard caps for critical gaps) and `automation-audit-ops` (evidence-first, read-only inventory of every automation in the repo — GitHub Actions, Claude Code hooks/settings, MCP servers, composer scripts, the bundled installer, the skills catalog, scheduler — classified live/broken/redundant with keep/merge/cut/fix recommendations). **Testing & performance:** `benchmark` (measure page Core Web Vitals, API latency percentiles, build/test velocity, and DB query timing as git-tracked baselines) and `benchmark-optimization-loop` (turn a vague speed goal into a bounded, measured loop that promotes only verified, correctness-preserving wins via a baseline, a variant ledger, and a promotion gate; defers measurement mechanics to `@skills/benchmark/SKILL.md`). **Platform & Data:** `postgres-patterns` (the PostgreSQL counterpart to `mysql-patterns` — GIN/BRIN/partial/covering indexes, `jsonb`, `ON CONFLICT` upserts, `SKIP LOCKED` queue workers, cursor pagination, RLS, `timestamptz`/`numeric` typing, all in Laravel terms). Additionally, the existing `frontend-a11y` skill was **extended** with the net-new WCAG 2.2 success criteria from the ECC `accessibility` skill, adapted to Blade/Tailwind/Livewire: target size (SC 2.5.8), focus appearance (SC 2.4.11), redundant entry (SC 3.3.7), dragging-movement alternatives (SC 2.5.7), and error suggestions (SC 3.3.3). The ECC `accessibility` skill was folded into `frontend-a11y` rather than duplicated; iOS/Android/React content was dropped as out-of-stack. README skill count bumped 49 → 57. Deferred (out of scope): the pre-existing `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` `body.max_tokens` warning (8756 > 5000 tokens) — a non-trivial rewrite of an unrelated skill. Per issue #571 — *implementuj tyto skilly do vhodných skilů projektu nebo vytvoř nové tak, aby dodržovali konvence skillů*.
- ✨ **Added**: first-class Bugsnag tracker support, bringing Bugsnag to parity with the GitHub and JIRA flows. Until now Bugsnag was a pass-through (the detection table only said *"treat as runtime error, prefer TDD"* and reports were redirected to a mirrored GitHub issue); there was no loader, no comment publisher, and no direct API contact. This change adds a deterministic script pair under the new `skills/code-review-bugsnag/scripts/` directory, mirroring `code-review-github/scripts/` and `code-review-jira/scripts/` exactly. **`load-issue.sh <URL|TRIPLE>`** is the single deterministic entry point for Bugsnag context: it accepts an `app.bugsnag.com/<org>/<project>/errors/<id>` URL or an `<org>/<project>/<error-id>` triple, reads a Data Access API token from `BUGSNAG_TOKEN` (alias `BUGSNAG_AUTH_TOKEN`; never read from a file, never written anywhere), resolves the dashboard slugs to numeric API ids (org slug → org id → project slug → project id, paginating projects via the `Link: rel="next"` header), then fetches the error, its comments, and its latest event, and emits one stable JSON document — `errorClass` / `message` / `context` / `status` / `severity` / `events` / `firstSeen` / `lastSeen`, `linkedIssues[]` (the mirrored GitHub issue/PR), `comments[]`, `groupingFields`, and a slimmed `latestEvent` carrying the in-project `stacktrace` frames (the entry point for a TDD reproduction), app / device / request / user context, and breadcrumbs. **`upsert-comment.sh <URL|TRIPLE> -`** is the append-only comment publisher (same always-new-comment-per-run model as the GitHub helper; Bugsnag renders plain text, so no hidden per-actor marker — the token identifies the author). Both scripts use `curl` + `jq`, the `Authorization: token <TOKEN>` + `X-Version: 2` headers, and the deterministic exit-code contract (`1` usage, `2` missing tool / token, `3` API failure). The requiring skills now consume this logic: `skills/resolve-issue/references/source-detection.md` points the Bugsnag row at the loader (Bugsnag MCP fallback), `skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md` gains a Bugsnag project-ownership check (via `linkedIssues[]`), a Bugsnag loader branch in the fetch step, Bugsnag in the read-`comments[]`-directly step, a non-technical report posted **directly on the Bugsnag error** (mirrored to the linked GitHub issue), and a Bugsnag-specific follow-up that — like JIRA status transitions — leaves marking an error *fixed* to a human; `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` and `skills/assignment-compliance-check/SKILL.md` gain Bugsnag loader branches (the error class / message / context / stacktrace is the assignment, the linked GitHub issue supplies any human-authored acceptance criteria). A new read-only wrapper `skills/code-review-bugsnag/SKILL.md` (modeled on `code-review-jira`) runs the standard sub-review chain on the linked PR, posts technical findings on the GitHub PR, and delegates the non-technical summary to `@skills/pr-summary/SKILL.md` posted on the Bugsnag error. The design rationale and step plan are captured in `docs/plans/bugsnag-integration.md`. README skill count bumped 48 → 49. Per user request — *navrhni a zprovozni řešení pro Bugsnag, které umí všechno co github a jira skills, ideálně přes CLI/MCP; ověř komunikaci a uprav požadující skilly*.
- 📝 **Changed**: query changes must not regress performance. `@rules/sql/optimalize.mdc` gains a **Performance Non-Regression on Query Changes** section requiring every refactored or changed query to capture the original's `EXPLAIN` baseline and be equal or faster — and, when measurably slower, to document why, the remaining optimization options (or that none exist and why), and the trade-off that justifies it. Wired into the DB-query skills that change or refactor queries: `mysql-problem-solver` (baseline-vs-rewrite gate + a Performance Comparison block in the analysis report template), `mysql-patterns`, `class-refactoring` (query performance is part of the behavior-preservation contract), `laravel-telescope`, and the `code-review` SQL review pass (Database Analysis finding).
- ✨ **Added**: 15 new Agent skills adapted from the [ECC](https://github.com/affaan-m/ECC) skill collection, each rewritten for this package's Laravel 11 / PHP 8.3 / Filament / Livewire / Alpine / (condensed secure-defaults reference — auth, authorization, Eloquent safety, CSRF/XSS, API security, file uploads, secrets, production hardening) and `security-bounty-hunter` (exploit-oriented live-region management, Filament a11y), `vite-patterns` (laravel-vite-plugin entrypoints, `@vite` directive, HMR, manifests, production builds), `seo` (crawlability, JSON-LD in Blade, Core Web Vitals, on-page tags), and `frontend-slides` (self-contained HTML/CSS/JS decks with browser Print-to-PDF, no external tooling). **Delivery:** `git-workflow` (branching strategy, merge vs rebase, conflicts, stash, undo, release tagging — defers commit/PR conventions to `@rules/git/general.mdc`, branch cleanup to `@skills/cleanup-local-branches/SKILL.md`, merging to `@skills/merge-github-pr/SKILL.md`). README skill count bumped 33 → 48 with a new **Frontend & UI** catalog section. - 🗑️ **Removed**: the bundled Claude Code subagents feature. The installer no longer copies them: `Installer::collectSyncPayloads()` drops the agents payload and `InstallerPath` loses `resolveAgentsSource()` / `resolveAgentsTargetDirectories()` / `isAgentsEditor()`; `--editor=claude` / `--editor=all` installs now mirror only `rules/` and `skills/` (plus `CLAUDE.md`). The README **Claude Code Subagents** section and the corresponding Pest lock-in tests in `tests/InstallerTest.php` are removed. The same workflows remain available directly as slash-command skills.
